#5201. Bitcoin Mining Legend Chun Wang Rebuys ETH at 14% Discount After $18.5 Million Sale

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

F2Pool co-founder Chun Wang, known on X as Satofishi, bought 5,246 ETH worth $10.78 million over the past two days at an average price of $2,054, according to Lookonchain and Arkham data.

On Nov. 5, with ether trading near $2,400, Wang sold 6,200 wrapped staked ETH (wstETH) for $18.5 million in USDC. Wrapped staked ETH is a version of Lido's staked ether (stETH) that can be used in DeFi protocols.

This week, ether traded about 14% lower than on the day of the sale, and Wang started buying it back.

He withdrew 5,246 ETH from Binance hot wallets in two transfers of 3,996 ETH and 1,250 ETH. The purchase cost him $10.78 million, so about $7.7 million from the Nov. 5 sale is still left in USDC, according to Lookonchain.

Wang then sent 3,996 ETH ($8.23 million) to Lido. Lido is the largest liquid staking protocol on Ethereum. He deposited the wstETH he received into Spark, a lending protocol in the Sky ecosystem, which was previously known as MakerDAO. In Spark, wstETH can be used as collateral, while it continues to earn staking rewards.

He started out arbitraging Bitcoin between overseas exchanges and China's Taobao and later co-founded F2Pool. He launched the pool in 2013 together with Mao Shixing. At that time, F2Pool was one of the first Bitcoin mining pools in China.

In 2015, he bought a condo in Pattaya for 2,900 BTC. He later sold it for 7 BTC, as the price of Bitcoin had risen many times over.

In 2025, Wang also funded and commanded Fram2, the first crewed spaceflight over Earth's polar regions, on a SpaceX Crew Dragon.

F2Pool also mined ether until Ethereum moved to proof-of-stake in September 2022. Before that, Wang launched the non-custodial staking service stakefish. According to Wang, he decided to do it after a conversation with Vitalik Buterin in 2017. Stakefish was also one of the first node operators of Lido.

Ether is down more than 15% since the start of the month amid outflows from spot ETFs, according to SoSoValue. At press time, ETH is trading at $2,020.

#5200. Why Strategy’s MSCI removal could spell trouble for Bitcoin’s Q4 outlook

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

If you thought Q4 volatility was finally cooling down, you might want to think again.

And yes, the December rate decision priced in by the market is only the tip of the iceberg. Analysts have spent much of the last 24 hours assessing the MSCI report announcing its index review on the 11th of November. Naturally, the focus immediately shifted to Strategy’s MSTR index eligibility as traders began pricing in the risk of its removal from MSIC’s global indexes. Notably, this is exactly how things played out.

In its recent review, MSCI has officially confirmed that Strategy will be removed from its global indexes, effective from the 1st of December. This put MSTR among the companies set to lose their place in these benchmarks, adding another layer of uncertainty around institutional exposure and potential selling pressure.

However, to understand why this decision matters, it is important to first understand what MSCI represents.

MSCI (Morgan Stanley Capital International) creates some of the biggest stock indices in the world. Investment funds that have over $21 trillion in assets track these indices. Many invest in stocks according to these indices. If the fund tracks an MSCI index including MSTR, then the Strategy removal may cause these investment funds to decrease or sell their holdings to match the new index.

It, therefore, comes as little surprise that traders are worried that MSTR’s removal by MSCI will cause a wave of institutional selling. Moreover, given that Strategy is still the largest corporate holder of Bitcoin [BTC] (850,364 BTC on their balance sheet), the importance of this decision cannot be understated. Less institutional exposure to MSTR could cause sentiment to turn more negative towards corporate Bitcoin treasuries.

Traders are now watching whether the removal triggers a broader risk-off move across the crypto market.

Following MSCI’s decision, MSTR’s institutional ownership is becoming an important part of the story.

Some of Wall Street’s largest asset management firms also hold large stakes within the company, such as Capital International Investors, BlackRock, Morgan Stanley, Invesco, and Goldman Sachs. Based on the latest institutional filings, it can be seen that Capital International Investors is the largest institutional investor holding around 34.9 million MSTR shares. This is followed by BlackRock with 19.39 million shares.

Interestingly, institutional investment increased within Q2. Invesco increased their shareholding by around 7.21 million shares. Meanwhile, Goldman Sachs increased theirs by 4.02 million shares, Capital International Investors increased theirs by 3.46 million shares and BlackRock increased their shareholding by around 1.64 million shares. Overall, there were around 209.52 million MSTR shares owned by institutional investors at the end of Q2, an increase of 9.62% compared to the previous quarter.

However, not every major institution was accumulating.

The move by Capital Research Global Investors, who shed around 4.62 million shares, and Bank of America, who sold 2.79 million, demonstrates that it was already a slightly negative time for institutions. The primary concern therefore will be whether MSCI’s decision to exclude MSTR may speed up institutional selling.

In this context, MSCI’s latest decision could become a turning point for Bitcoin’s late Q4 rally. If the funds tracking MSCI’s indices are forced to sell their MSTR stake, it will add more downward pressure. It may also damage institutional confidence in Strategy’s “high-risk, Bitcoin-focused” investment strategy. In turn, creating further uncertainty in the crypto markets.

MSCI’s removal could force index-tracking funds to sell MSTR shares, adding further selling pressure.

Despite rising institutional holdings in Q2, the decision could hurt confidence in Strategy and other corporate Bitcoin holders.

#5199. Google’s new AI reveals hidden genetic clues in new immune-cell test

AlphaGenome 2 tailors predictions to cell types and states, with DeepMind reporting benchmark gains and reproduction of established disease biology.

Google DeepMind announced AlphaGenome 2 on Nov. 11, 2026, a DNA prediction model designed to show how genetic changes act in specific cell types and states. The approach aims to expose disease-linked effects that broad tissue measurements can obscure, giving scientists a more focused starting point for experiments.

The original AlphaGenome produced fixed parallel outputs across tissues and experiments from a million-letter DNA sequence. AlphaGenome 2 instead takes a target condition, such as a tissue, cell type or cell state, alongside the sequence and uses that condition throughout its architecture, according to DeepMind.

Its announcement pairs a reported improvement on an immune-cell benchmark with a computational reproduction of an established bowel-disease mechanism. The examples show how the model could focus laboratory work on particular variants and cells.

That allows predictions to ask how the same stretch of DNA behaves in different cellular settings. DeepMind says this can help identify effects confined to particular cells that broader tissue models miss. A tissue-level result can provide useful context while still leaving the cell responsible for a disease-linked effect unresolved.

AlphaGenome 2 combines DNA with a target cell type or state to predict effects in that setting.

Cell-specific predictions can focus experiments on effects broad tissue averages may miss.

Company-reported research predictions. The ETS2 mechanism predates this model. AlphaGenome 2 has not been validated or approved for clinical or diagnostic use.

Qualitative input-to-prediction schematic using the reported model design, plus DeepMind's retrospective ETS2 example. Shapes, distances and cell symbols encode relationships, not measured signal magnitudes, cell counts or effect sizes.

Company-reported research predictions. The ETS2 mechanism predates this model. AlphaGenome 2 has not been validated or approved for clinical or diagnostic use.

The company reports training the model on bulk and single-cell data from human and mouse samples, including more than 107 million individual cell profiles. The profiles supply training material for learning how DNA activity varies across biological settings.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

DeepMind also says researchers can supply single-cell gene-activity data to condition predictions on newly observed states without retraining the model. The system can then predict properties that were not measured, helping scientists decide which experiments to prioritize.

On TenK10K, a dataset covering 28 primary immune-cell types, DeepMind reports that AlphaGenome 2 identified 30% of causal variants in the evaluation, compared with 16% for the original AlphaGenome fine-tuned on single-cell data.

These developer-reported benchmark results measure variant identification, not diagnostic accuracy or a person’s disease risk.

For researchers, the practical attraction is a larger set of candidate variants to investigate in the cells where they may act. Connecting a disease-associated genetic signal to a particular variant, target gene and cell type can make the next experiment more focused.

The bowel-disease example makes the distinction concrete. In its Nov. 11 announcement, DeepMind says AlphaGenome 2 computationally reproduced a finding involving a regulatory variant that increases activity of the ETS2 gene in gut macrophages, a type of immune cell.

The model singled out the variant and predicted its selective effect on ETS2 in those macrophages, with other cell types and genes largely unaffected. The useful clue is the combination of variant, gene and cell type: it points to a specific cellular setting for investigating the effect.

The underlying biology predates this model. In a June 7, 2024 report, Imperial College London described research conducted with the Francis Crick Institute and UCL that linked a macrophage-active regulatory region to increased ETS2 activity and inflammatory functions. The biological mechanism was therefore already established; AlphaGenome 2's contribution in this example is reproducing it computationally.

As of the Nov. 11 announcement, DeepMind offered AlphaGenome 2 through its API for non-commercial research, with commercial research in private preview. The company states that both AlphaGenome models are intended for research and have not been validated or approved for clinical or diagnostic purposes.

The immediate opportunity is to choose better-targeted experiments: investigate a genetic change in the cell type or state where its effect is predicted to appear.

#5198. Bottomline Completes First USDC Transfers Inside Its Enterprise Payments Platform

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Bottomline has completed live USDC transfers inside its enterprise payments platform. The initial transfer was between company entities.

Bottomline disclosed the cross-border transactions in a November 10 announcement, using its Global Payments Hub and Cash Management platform.

The practical point is where the payment fits. A finance team may be more interested in approving, tracking and reconciling a transfer within its existing systems than in opening a separate crypto application to make it happen.

Bottomline describes the milestone as an intra-company transfer. That establishes that the company used the integration for a live movement of funds; it does not establish that unrelated businesses are already using it at scale.

The release says the aim is to let treasury teams handle stablecoin payments alongside traditional methods, retaining familiar approvals, controls and audit processes. It presents near-real-time settlement for selected scenarios, especially cross-border payments, as part of the attraction.

The company has not supplied a transaction-by-transaction performance dataset in this announcement. Readers therefore cannot use it to calculate an average fee saving or a reliable improvement in end-to-end delivery time.

Bottomline’s cash-management product description emphasises connecting treasury, accounting, compliance, banking and enterprise resource planning data. It describes tools for cash visibility, reconciliation and reporting, helping place the USDC announcement within the wider product.

Consider a group with businesses in two countries. Moving value between them is only one part of the job. Staff may also need to identify the correct entities, record the purpose of the transfer, obtain approval and match the completed payment to the accounts.

If a stablecoin route requires those steps to be reconstructed manually outside the normal workflow, a fast blockchain transfer can still leave an expensive administrative task. Integration is useful when it keeps the financial record attached to the movement of money.

NewsBTC explored a related implementation issue in Circle and ACI’s Latin American settlement collaboration, which examined how a digital-dollar option fits within existing payment operations.

Circle describes USDC as a dollar-redeemable token backed by liquid reserves. That explains the asset being transferred, but a corporate payment can involve more than the onchain movement itself.

A business funding a transfer from a bank account needs an entry route. A recipient that pays suppliers in local currency may need conversion and a bank payout. Each stage can affect the amount received and the time before it can be spent.

For that reason, the useful comparison is between two complete payment routes serving the same need. A low network charge does not settle the comparison if conversion costs are higher, or if an exception requires substantial staff time to resolve.

The next useful disclosures would identify supported use cases, the businesses choosing them and performance over repeated transfers. Clear handling of failures, rejected payments and reconciliation exceptions would be particularly informative.

There is also a difference between a first transaction and a mature operating metric, as NewsBTC’s analysis of Bitso Business payment volumes explains. Neither measure should be mistaken for fee revenue or universal customer availability.

Bottomline has demonstrated a concrete integration step. Its commercial value will come from making a recurring treasury task easier to complete and account for, with enough transparency for finance teams to compare the new route with the options they already trust.

written by the News Desk and edited by Samuel Rae.

#5197. Binance Wallet to End Support for Ethereum Layer 2 Blast

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

In a recent announcement, Binance Wallet stated it will discontinue support for the Blast chain effective November 30, 2026, at 10:00 (UTC). After this date, the crypto exchange said that users will no longer be able to view, send, or receive Blast assets within the Binance Wallet.

Ethereum layer-2 network Blast announced in October 2026 that it was shutting down, a little over two years after its launch, as fading activity left it unable to cover operating costs.

Binance noted that this change only affects the display and management of Blast assets within Binance Wallet, as users can still claim their assets on the official Blast site. In light of this, users are strongly advised to manage or transfer their Blast assets before November 30, 2026, at 10:00 (UTC).

This update only affects the Blast network and does not impact other blockchains supported by Binance Wallet.

Binance has resumed its cryptocurrency listing activity after a brief pause, introducing new perpetual contracts for Hyperliquid (HYPE) and TRON (TRX).

The late-October hack had interrupted the exchange's listings; its last addition was on October 29, when it announced it was listing the Predict token.

In the last 24 hours, Binance has made two listing announcements: one for HYPE and TRX perpetual contracts, and one for TradFi perpetual contracts on Nu Holdings Ltd. Class A Ordinary Shares and Freedom Holding Corp. Common Stock.

Binance Futures will launch HYPEUSDC and TRXUSDC Perpetual Contracts with up to 75x leverage on November 16, 2026, at 08:30 (UTC) and 08:35 (UTC), respectively. The move is intended to expand the list of trading choices offered on Binance Futures and enhance users’ trading experience.

To expand the list of trading choices offered on Binance Futures and enhance users’ trading experience, the NUUSDT USDT-Priced Perpetual Contract and the FRHCUSDT USDT-Priced Perpetual Contract have been admitted to trading on Binance RIE and to clearing and settlement by Binance RCH today, November 12, 2026.

#5196. PEPE Sits 25% From Overtaking SHIB By Market Cap

Home » Altcoins » PEPE Sits 25% From Overtaking SHIB By Market Cap

Pepe’s lagging behind Shiba Inu by a historically-near distance, but the broader market takes a toll on both.

The old-timer meme coins Shiba Inu (SHIB) & PEPE Token (PEPE) have been competing for the top spot in the global meme currency charts. Presently, PEPE Coin’s (PEPE) market cap stands at $1.86 billion, while Shiba Inu’s (SHIB) hovers around $2.53 billion, says SoSoValue.

There’s a huge discrepancy between the trading volumes, as PEPE Coin (PEPE) currently edges Shiba Inu’s daily turnover by a ten-fold distance. Despite today’s 11.29% downturn for the frog-embossed memecoin, PEPE garnered $470 million in contrast to SHIB’s $43.8 million.

Two days ago, PEPE Token (PEPE) leapfrogged to its monthly peak at $0.00000562, scoring a 14.4% rally while the broader market carried on gradually dipping. Amid those gains, PEPE emerged as the biggest meme coin by trading volumes, even overtaking Dogecoin (DOGE).

Pepe has a market cap of about $2.11 billion against $2.61 billion for Shiba Inu per @coingecko, and $PEPE would need a 24% to 25% rise to pass it. pic.twitter.com/yYuZOU4ehv

The speculative interest on Perpetual markets has also witnessed a considerable spike. With the general market soaking in fear at ‘29’ on Crypto’s Fear & Greed Index, PEPE coin’s price positions have flipped towards bearish levels. The long versus short ratio now flashes 0.90.

That means the short-sellers are outnumbering the market participants betting on the meme coin’s price ascension. The $2.26 million in excessively-leveraged position liquidations hints at PEPE’s holders expecting a PEPE price rally, while short-sellers incurred just $187,000 of it.

Conversely, the top-trader accounts on Derivatives have kept strongly above 1, registering a 1.089 ratio on OKX exchange. The strong belief came weeks after Canary Capital amended their first-ever PEPE ETF, tracking the Spot-market price of the cult-like meme currency.

The previous rally was partly sparked by the news of Justin Sun becoming the top PEPE Coin holder. Arkham data links $51.25 million of PEPE, about 2.82% of supply, to crypto wallets tied to Justin Sun, making him the largest individual holder as PEPE sparked up a 33.5% rally.

🚨 NEW: Justin Sun holds more $PEPE than any other individual, Arkham reports.His official and Arkham-predicted wallets collectively hold nearly 3% of PEPE’s supply, worth $51.25 MILLION, following massive inflows over the past 10 days.Two withdrawals from Poloniex alone… pic.twitter.com/f8Q61TBiCk

In spite of that, PEPE Coin (PEPE) now stands out as the biggest loser on Thursday, driving the price lower by more than 11% on the daily time-frame. The ability to stay above $0.00000444 is of key importance: with much of last month’s rally tracing back, PEPE relies on demand areas.

These are usually captured by the crypto whales, big-time investors who tend to enter during a local price bottom. This time, the Chaikin Money Flow (CMF) points to hefty selling going on, as the CMF index hovers between -0.17 to -0.30 on time-frames ranging from one-hour to one-day.

#5195. USDT fell below $1 – That’s how thin crypto liquidity has gotten

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

The crypto market is at a critical point with its stablecoin liquidity continuing to shrink as the year nears its end. Over the past four weeks, more than $14.20 billion in stablecoins have left the market.

Currently, the stablecoin market cap has declined below $300 billion, which is its lowest level in 14 months. That is, the cap plunged from $313 billion to $298 billion, the fastest contraction since June 2022.

Additionally, Tether’s USDT was trading below $1 at the time of press, showing that the stablecoin’s buying power was weakening too.

This contraction implies that crypto liquidity in the market is thin and fresh capital is limited to support a bullish recovery. Both Bitcoin [BTC] and Ethereum [ETH] have been averagely on a decline when gauging this month’s performance.

This suggests that both BTC and the broader altcoin market are bearish. Thus, the stablecoin supply did not necessarily go into buying and holding like in the previous years.

Meanwhile, Bitcoin’s price remained heavily influenced by liquidation-driven moves rather than organic spot demand. As per the Binance Liquidation Accumulation Index, the volatility was driven by two-way liquidations.

Between September and November, the index has visited the upper and lower bands, at 80 and 20, respectively, more than three times each. This implies that both longs and shorts are being wiped out at this juncture.

With that, the price of BTC could continue bouncing between $60K and $87K until either bears or bulls win the battle.

At the same time, Spot demand was struggling to provide consistent support.

For instance, Binance Buy/Sell Delta has declined from 0.07 to below the neutral level, affirming the weak spot demand. In fact, traders on the exchange were selling.

Additionally, U.S. Spot Bitcoin ETFs saw net outflows of over $1.60 billion over the past 20 sessions. Over the past 24 hours, around $299.90 million exited the BTC ETFs market.

Similarly, Ethereum and Solana ETFs lost $144.81 million and $5.85 million, respectively, while those of Binance Coin [BNB] and XRP remained inactive.

Therefore, if crypto liquidity and spot demand do not return, then a general market recovery would be hard to materialize. Thus, a turnback to stablecoin market cap growth would be the first sign of new money.

#5194. Solana (SOL) to Connect With Wall Street’s $4.7 Quadrillion Clearing Giant

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

The Depository Trust & Clearing Corporation (DTCC) will connect its tokenization service to Solana (SOL) in the second half of 2027, the company said on Nov. 12.

The integration will allow "digital twins" of Russell 1000 stocks, major ETFs and U.S. Treasuries to be issued on the Solana blockchain. In December 2025, the SEC issued a no-action letter that allowed DTC to tokenize these assets.

In 2025, DTCC processed $4.7 quadrillion in securities transactions. Its depository, DTC, holds and services $114 trillion in assets from more than 150 countries.

The DTCC Tokenization Service (DTS) will let large market participants convert conventional securities into tokens, move them across the Solana network and redeem them.

"DTC-tokenized assets made available on Solana – and all blockchain networks integrated with the service – will retain the same investor protections, ownership rights and regulatory safeguards that apply to traditionally held securities," said Brian Steele, president of DTCC Clearing & Securities Services.

According to DTCC CEO Frank La Salla, the integration will also free up securities for use as collateral.

DTS went live on Oct. 28, 2026. At launch, it worked with Canton Network and a private ledger. DTCC said that it adds new networks at clients' request.

"DTCC is committed to providing an open and connected platform that provides our users with access to mature distributed ledger technology networks like Solana that have established performance, security and governance features," said Nadine Chakar, global head of DTCC Digital Assets.

Nick Ducoff, general manager for institutional at the Solana Foundation, also commented on the news. "Institutions choose Solana because it already runs real financial activity at scale," he said.

According to Ducoff, the integration "connects one of the most trusted names in post-trade infrastructure with a network built for always-on markets."

As of Nov. 12, distributed real-world assets (RWA) in the Solana ecosystem reached $5.13 billion across more than 1.02 million holders, according to RWA.xyz. Transfers over the past 30 days totaled $16.48 billion.

The stablecoin market cap on Solana stands at $13.85 billion, with monthly turnover of $387.41 billion.

#5193. Zcash loses $1,000 as dormant wallets send ZEC coins to exchanges

Kairos Research found that traders exiting bullish positions drove the initial selloff, with fresh token deposits raising the risk of further losses.

Zcash fell below $1,000 as leveraged traders abandoned bullish positions and selling pressure intensified.

Data from CoinMarketCap showed ZEC at approximately $965 on Nov. 12, after a 24-hour low near $953.

The decline has exposed vulnerabilities in ZEC's market structure, with research showing heavy selling across derivatives exchanges and fresh deposits from previously inactive wallets.

A market investigation by Kairos Research identified the rapid closure of a 12,500 ZEC long position on Hyperliquid as a pivotal moment in the selloff.

The position, accumulated in the hour following the release of US inflation data, was closed through market orders within two seconds at 15:29:58 UTC on Nov. 10, just as Bitcoin began retreating.

ZEC subsequently fell from $1,193 to $1,001 as traders sold a net 160,000 ZEC across four perpetual futures exchanges over the following 90 minutes.

Binance spot hourly-closing price represented in the Kairos Research graphic. Binance accounted for 74,000 ZEC, followed by OKX with 42,000, Hyperliquid with 31,000, and Bybit with 13,000. Spot markets recorded approximately 27,000 ZEC in net taker selling.

Kairos found that traders primarily drove the selling by unwinding existing bullish positions rather than forced liquidations.

On Hyperliquid, taker sales associated with closing longs reached 109,000 ZEC between 14:30 and 17:00 UTC, compared with 50,000 ZEC from traders opening shorts. Forced liquidations accounted for only 6,900 ZEC during that period.

Coinbase had already recorded approximately 3,000 ZEC in net spot selling before the breakdown, while derivatives traders on Binance, OKX and Hyperliquid remained net buyers.

The researchers also traced Coinbase deposits and found that only about 12,000 ZEC had arrived across Nov. 9 and 10, suggesting most of the initial spot selling came from inventory already held on the exchange.

However, fresh deposits accelerated after the decline.

By 18:00 UTC on Nov. 11, Coinbase had received another 16,300 ZEC, including 9,800 ZEC from three wallets that had been inactive since withdrawing funds from Coinbase Prime in November and December 2025.

Those wallets still held approximately 19,000 ZEC, while Binance received another 8,600 ZEC in large deposits over the same period.

The combined 24,900 ZEC in new exchange deposits raises the prospect of additional spot selling, although transfers alone do not establish an intention to liquidate.

The selling pressure has also extended to Grayscale's Zcash ETF, ZCSH, which is heading toward its second consecutive week of net withdrawals.

Data from SoSoValue showed that the fund recorded approximately $13 million in outflows during the week ending Nov. 6, followed by another $17 million this week.

The combined withdrawals are approaching $30 million.

The fund's cumulative net inflows have fallen to $192 million, while total net assets have also declined to $562 million from about $772 million at the end of the previous week.

However, Kairos found no evidence that fresh ETF redemptions caused the initial Nov. 10 selloff.

ZCSH traded 2.46 million shares that day without recording new creations or redemptions, while 1,861 ZEC transferred into Coinbase Prime represented settlement of an earlier withdrawal.

In a research report, the asset manager said Zcash's monthly active developer population had roughly doubled over two years, while developer participation in the broader cryptocurrency industry has retreated from its previous peak.

The firm also found that approximately 70% of Zcash's active developers had more than one year of cryptocurrency development experience, compared with 58% across the industry.

The bullish outlook also reflects progress in the network's development.

Zcash activated its NU7 upgrade on Nov. 6, reducing its target block interval from 75 seconds to 25 seconds and introducing changes designed to improve private payment processing.

Meanwhile, data from Zcash's shielded supply tracker showed that approximately 5 million ZEC, representing more than 29% of circulating supply, was held in shielded pools as of Nov. 10.

Grayscale has reinforced its bullish thesis with a $1 million commitment to the Zcash ecosystem, including $400,000 already awarded to four development and advocacy organizations.

The firm also launched a nationwide advertising campaign featuring a prominent Wall Street Journal promotion and a dedicated investment education platform.

It intends to reinvest sponsor fees collected from ZCSH into network development, marketing, and education for up to the fund's first year as an exchange-traded product.

Nonetheless, Grayscale's investments and Zcash's growing developer community have yet to reverse the deterioration in investor demand. With the fund approaching $30 million in withdrawals over two weeks and previously inactive wallets still holding another 19,000 ZEC, renewed selling could further test the cryptocurrency's ability to recover above $1,000.

#5192. NEAR Intents fuels $2.7M in Q4 buybacks as revenue surges

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

NEAR Protocol [NEAR] is in the news for all the good reasons. According to reports, the decentralized finance (DeFi) ecosystem of NEAR is seeing increased activity through its cross-chain swap product, NEAR Intents, which has generated enough revenue to fund nearly $2.7 million worth of NEAR token buybacks in the Q4 of 2026.

Interestingly, this figure is already close to the $3.07 million spent on buybacks in Q3 2026, although half of Q4 is still left. So for those unaware, the buyback program began in February 2026. Since then, the protocol has purchased approximately $8 million worth of NEAR tokens.

In fact, so far now, NEAR Intents has collected approximately $8.68 million in fees since the 1st of October. Of this amount, around $2.82 million has been retained by the protocol, while the remaining $5.86 million has gone to solvers and partner applications.

According to Token Terminal data, revenue started around $315K in December 2025, climbed to a first peak of roughly $740K in February 2026, then fell sharply to around $200-240K between April and July, before rebuilding through the autumn to $650K in September. Finally, it crossed the $1 million mark in October.

This means that NEAR Intents is the main revenue driver, contributing roughly 74% of October’s total and the bulk of revenue in nearly every month, while the base protocol’s contribution stays relatively small and steady at around $30-260K.

That said, the platform recorded $3.76 billion in swap volume over the past 30 days, taking its cumulative volume since launch to approximately $37.3 billion. However, sustained buyback activity does not necessarily guarantee price appreciation.

The recent price action of NEAR trading at ​​$3.51 after a drop of 2.6% in the past 24 hours reflected the same. At the same time, the NEAR ETFs also saw outflows worth $5.74 million in the month of November so far.

Yet despite these weak market dynamics, the total value locked (TVL) in NEAR Intents’ confidential pools stood at $140.79 million at press time, up 402.7% over the last 90 days.

That said, the TVL started around $27 million in mid-August and grew steadily through early September, then surged sharply in mid-to-late September, peaking at roughly $185 million around 25-26 September.

After that peak, it pulled back and settled into a fairly stable range of about 135 million to 165 million through October and early November, with a minor dip near the end of October. In fact, according to a recent report by AMBCrypto, NEAR Intents recorded $10.41 billion in Q3 trading volume, up 66% quarter-on-quarter and 891% year-on-year.

This, however, coincided with North Korean hackers linked to the $6.3 billion Binance exploit using six memecoins created on NEAR to launder stolen cryptocurrency, moving approximately 117,000 NEAR worth $430,000.

Decentralized exchange Hyperliquid has bought back and burned 21,200 HYPE over the past 24 hours, spending $1.53 million on the purchase at an average price of $72.31, according to Onchain Lens.

The on-chain analytics platform also reported that Hyperliquid has permanently removed a total of 50.47 million HYPE from circulation since the launch of the token. That is 5.05% of the max supply of 1 billion HYPE, and at the current price, the burned tokens are worth around $3.51 billion.

The latest buyback comes amid the decline in the price of the cryptocurrency. On Nov. 12, the price of HYPE went down by 3.45%, and at press time, the token stands at $69.52, according to TradingView. Earlier this autumn, the price of HYPE was above $90.

The Assistance Fund buys HYPE on the open market and sends the tokens to the "0xfefe...fefe" address. It is a system address that does not have a private key. In December 2025, Hyperliquid validators formally recognized the tokens held at this address as burned.

In August 2026, Hyperliquid also introduced the AQAv2 upgrade. Under the upgrade, around 90% of the yield generated by the USDC reserves on the platform now goes to the Assistance Fund. Each of these transfers brings between $14 million and $16 million to the fund.

Besides the Assistance Fund, HYPE is also burned through network fees. Around 1.24 million HYPE has been burned through fees and slashing on HyperCore, the base layer of Hyperliquid. Another 376,060 HYPE has been burned as gas fees on HyperEVM, the smart contract layer of the network.

#5190. Near AI Breach Exposes User Details and Data From 18 AI Agents

Near AI Breach Exposes User Details and Data From 18 AI Agents

Near AI has disclosed a breach that exposed account information belonging to about 100 users and data from 18 AI agents after an attacker exploited software running its servers.

The company said in a November 9 report that an attacker used a remote code execution vulnerability in SGLang, an open-source engine that runs AI models and generates responses to user requests.

The exploit allowed the intruder to execute commands on one inference server and then reach other parts of Near AI’s infrastructure.

Investigators traced the intrusion to an internal system connected to Private Chat, a feature the company had already retired. The attacker downloaded names and email addresses for approximately 100 users, along with subscription plan and account balance information for some of them.

Another 18 agents were accessed on an older deployment of Near AI Agents, where the attacker retrieved their chat histories, settings and logs. Those agents were not running inside NEAR AI’s confidential computing environment, which is designed to shield information from the company operating the servers.

Logs also exposed NEAR AI Cloud API keys belonging to two customers. These keys function like digital credentials that applications use to access cloud services; Near AI says it has revoked both, but did not say whether the attacker used them.

Near AI found no indication that stored Private Chat conversations or uploaded files had been accessed. The confirmed theft involved account records and legacy agent data, while exposure of live confidential requests remains a possibility identified by the investigation.

Hardware isolation does not guarantee that the software inside the protected environment is safe. “It does not protect against a vulnerability in the software running inside the environment,” Near AI acknowledged, explaining that the SGLang flaw let the attacker run code where confidential requests were being processed.

SGLang had already attracted scrutiny from security researchers. In July, Carnegie Mellon University’s CERT Coordination Center documented six vulnerabilities in the project, including bugs that could let attackers execute commands or expose credentials, depending on how servers were configured.

Near AI did not identify the specific SGLang flaw exploited in this incident, so there is no basis to link the breach to any of those earlier disclosures. It reported the vulnerability to SGLang’s developers.

Near, to its credit, was quick to fix the issues.

“On detection, we revoked all active sessions and administrative credentials, replaced all agent tokens, took Private Chat offline, and deployed a fix for the SGLang vulnerability across all inference servers,” the project said.

”We've completed the rotation of all remaining credentials and a full forensic review. We have notified users whose account details or agents we have confirmed were accessed and will make any regulatory notifications that are required.”

Near AI notified users whose account records or agents it confirmed were accessed and says it will make any required regulatory notifications. It also warned users to be wary of unsolicited messages referring to their Near AI accounts or balances, and contacted the owners of all 18 affected agents individually.

#5188. TSMC confirms data breach as hacker demands Bitcoin worth $215K — Details

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

2026 hasn’t been a great year as far as security is concerned. Whether it is Bitget or Binance, every single episode this year has simply highlighted how important it is.

It hasn’t been a great time for chipmakers around the world either. The latest news associated with Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Company or TSMC is a case in point.

TSMC is in the news today after hackers reportedly offered up private information linked to one of the company’s employees on an underground forum. According to the hacker, they have access to over 46,000 records right now. These records will be released publicly if the hackers aren’t paid 3 BTC or $214.7K within one week.

Here, it’s worth pointing out that the hacker in question isn’t exactly holding the information to ransom. At the time of writing, there hadn’t been any demands or efforts to blackmail. Instead, the hacker is looking for a buyer, any buyer, for the information in question.

For its part, TSMC acknowledged that “data of some current and former employees was disclosed without authorization.” However, the company was also quick to stress that there is no real evidence that any of its security systems were compromised. Its preliminary investigative report suggested that,

… this data matches the data in the system service of a certain cloud service provider.

TSMC also clarified that the data in question wasn’t associated or linked with any clients or production.

Right now, the popular chipmaker is working with cloud service providers and cybersecurity agencies to better understand what happened. It is also taking measures to make sure this doesn’t happen again.

Here, it is worth clarifying that the present case isn’t exactly a traditional ransomware attack. Instead, it’s a simple case of data breach since no malware was used and TSMC hasn’t been locked out of its system.

Still, this is an industry that isn’t exactly new to ransomware episodes. Back in 2023, for example, TSMC was targeted by the LockBit group who demanded $70M.

According to Black Kite’s ransomware report, for instance, there has been a 24% YoY increase in ransomware disclosures compared to the previous disclosure period. It also found,

The threat actor ecosystem grew right alongside the victim count, reaching 127 active groups by the close of the period and 146 by June 2026.

Finally, the report in question also revealed that the manufacturing sector accounted for around 22% of all ransomware attacks. Professional, scientific, and medical services accounted for another 18% of the total share. The ransomware breach that targeted DaVita Inc. back in 2025 and affected over 2.7M patients is perhaps the most famous example of the same.

The timing is interesting. Especially since news of this incident coincided with Nvidia hogging the headlines after CEO Jensen Huang resigned following an extramarital scandal. In fact, one can argue that the news has taken the spotlight off TSMC for now.

As expected, this has had a corresponding effect on the sector, related industries, and their performances. For instance, the likes of IREN, Core Scientific, and TeraWulf all fell on the charts in the hours following the aforementioned resignation.

#5187. XRP proposed lending vaults lock deposits and expose holders to risk

Proposed XRPL lending rules would lock XRP vault withdrawals during investment periods, exposing depositors to borrower repayment risk.

Proposed V1.1 rules for the XRP Ledger (XRPL) would require new loan brokers to use closed-ended native-lending vaults, forgoing withdrawals during the investment period and leaving depositors with shares exposed to borrower repayments.

In a Nov. 12 snapshot, XRPLWin showed LendingProtocol and LendingProtocolV1_1 each at 22 of 35 trusted-validator votes, with SingleAssetVault at 23 of 35. The explorer displayed a 29-vote threshold, and XRPL’s amendment rules require more than 80% support maintained for two weeks.

Under Ripple’s proposed V1.1 transaction rules, newly created loan brokers would have to attach to closed-ended vaults. Brokers created before that amendment activates could continue originating loans against open-ended vaults.

During subscription, depositors can add assets and withdraw, while both actions are blocked and capital can fund loans during investment. Redemption reopens withdrawals, while new deposits and new loans are barred.

The lending mechanism transfers requested principal to the borrower, excluding fees. Once XRP is lent, the depositor holds a claim through vault shares while the borrower controls the disbursed tokens.

A deposit made with already-owned XRP does not establish a market purchase, and an investment-period withdrawal lock also does not permanently immobilize the XRP passed to borrowers.

The amount held idle in vaults, the amount outstanding on loan, and what borrowers do with it would have to be measured separately before drawing conclusions about tradable supply.

XRPL’s design uses fixed-term, uncollateralized loans and off-chain underwriting, so holders take on exposure to borrowers’ ability to repay and the broker’s credit decisions.

In an XRP lending vault, borrower-paid XRP interest could increase the XRP claim represented by those shares, while losses could reduce it. That keeps the investment denominated in XRP without guaranteeing the number of tokens ultimately recovered.

A broker may deposit the same asset in a separate account to buffer defaults, but that protection is optional and configurable. Its presence does not guarantee repayment or eliminate depositor losses.

V1.1 would also put newly created vaults on cash-basis accounting, with interest becoming income when paid. Vaults created under the earlier whole-life model, which books scheduled interest at loan origination, would retain that accounting permanently.

The vault must permit transfers, and a recipient must be eligible to hold both the shares and the underlying asset. Private-vault credentials and applicable asset restrictions can prevent a transfer.

Even where ownership can move, a sale requires a willing buyer and an executable price.

Proposed V1.1 rules for new loan brokers would separate the depositor’s withdrawal rights from control of loan principal.

Deposits and withdrawals are open before the investment period.

Deposits and withdrawals are blocked while capital can fund loans.

Withdrawals reopen; no new deposits or new loans. Recovery depends on the vault’s assets.

An investment-period withdrawal lock does not permanently remove loaned XRP from circulation.

Native lending remains unactivated in the November 12 snapshot. The design shown is for closed-ended XRP vaults and new V1.1 brokers; legacy brokers can retain open-ended vaults. Credit cover is optional. Transferability does not establish a buyer or price, and redemption does not guarantee full recovery.

Native lending remains unactivated in the November 12 snapshot. The design shown is for closed-ended XRP vaults and new V1.1 brokers; legacy brokers can retain open-ended vaults. Credit cover is optional. Transferability does not establish a buyer or price, and redemption does not guarantee full recovery.

Arf’s Oct. 22 announcement with Ripple noted that its first phase would provide direct RLUSD working capital on XRPL, with a planned move to native lending infrastructure after amendment activation. Underwriting would remain off-chain, and RLUSD would be the asset disbursed and repaid.

For holders, the next tests are amendment activation, funded XRP vaults, borrower drawdowns, paid interest, defaults and repayments.

Successful share sales would separately show whether an early exit is usable. Until those outcomes are observable, the proposed bargain for a closed-ended XRP vault is a withdrawal commitment in exchange for credit exposure, with borrower performance determining the income.

#5186. MSCI Drops Saylor's Strategy From Its Index Amid BTC Sell-Off

Across the MSCI ACWI Index, 13 securities will be added and 100 deleted. The two additions to the MSCI World Index, measured by full company market capitalization, are Moderna (USA) and Aritzia (Canada).

The latest review follows the new rules proposed by MSCI in October, among which are new measures regarding crypto treasury holding companies.

MSCI evaluates whether a company maintains substantial operating assets and applies five financial ratio tests. In case a company fails to meet at least four criteria,

A company that fails at least four criteria across consecutive annual reporting periods may face removal from the MSCI Global Investable Market Indexes.

MSCI indexes track public companies based on market cap and investment risks. Global mutual funds and ETFs use these benchmarks to build their portfolios.

Dropping out of such index could influence investment flows as well as stock prices.

#5185. Russia Legalizes Crypto Trading While XRP Stands Apart

Home » Bitcoin (BTC) » Russia Legalizes Crypto Trading While XRP Stands Apart

Platforms are able to conduct business operations in crypto, offering retail knowledge-based accessibility.

Russia’s crypto bid is going at a faster pace since the fourth largest economy around the globe introduced crypto trading for retail & businesses. The new set of rules, coming from the Central Bank Of Russia, limits regular investors to a 300,000 rubles yearly cap (roughly $3,600).

This allows buying & holding Bitcoin (BTC), Ethereum (ETH) & USDT through regulated intermediaries (brokers, trust managers & licensed exchanges). Everyone must pass a knowledge test plus review risk disclosures before starting trading activities, while XRP is considered an asset for advanced users only.

The Moscow Exchange already lists cash-settled perpetual futures (XRP/USDF) and related indices tracking XRP prices. These are available only to qualified investors, settle in rubles (no actual XRP is delivered), and form part of a broader set of crypto derivatives with substantial turnover among Futures traders across the scene.

Russian Central Bank: Exchanges May Conduct Crypto Trading from November 22, Subject to Prior Approval of Trading Rules According to TASS, the Bank of Russia said Russian exchanges may begin offering cryptocurrency trading from November 22, 2026, provided they first agree on… pic.twitter.com/txoYyq5vfL

The fresh legal framework dates back to August. Then, Russia’s President Vladimir Putin signed the inaugural comprehensive cryptocurrency law (Federal Law No. 282-FZ, “On Digital Currencies and Digital Rights”), setting September 1, 2026 activation window: contrastingly to the entry-level investor restrictions, qualified ones have no limits.

Meanwhile, businesses face hard limits, needing to meet a minimum equity requirement (15 million rubles, roughly $187,000) to run crypto services or custody services. The regulatory framework is way more lenient on businesses conducting cross-border sanctions to dodge the 21-chapter European Union sanction packages.

The retail crypto trading gets legalized on November 22, 2026, the related Duma documents state. The approved exchanges & crypto brokers are obliged to report the trading data and results to the central bank in accordance with existing reporting procedures for XRP & others.

For XRP traders in the region, the MOEX XRP Futures index allows up to 5x leverage.

RUSSIA MADE A MASSIVE $XRP MOVE!! pic.twitter.com/dmuUaPnsuP

Paying with XRP or any other popular crypto coin at a local store is still off the table. Goods & services are prohibited as a result of a separate ban, pushing crypto adoption in the perspective of an investment vehicle rather than a means of payment.

Still, setting up state-surveilled crypto brokers is a pretty big move that could inspire other major economies to do the same.

#5183. Ethereum’s store-of-value narrative is gaining traction – From Joseph Lubin, at least

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Ethereum’s “store-of-value” narrative is gaining traction lately.

Looking at the broader picture, there are several factors that are leading to increased risk sentiment in the U.S. market.

Whether it be fears over an AI bubble, high-interest rates, CPI figures, or the aftermath of the mid-term elections, Bitcoin is heading toward its worst annual cycle since the 2022 bear market, with year-to-date losses approaching 17% in 2026.

Keeping this in mind, while Ethereum has suffered nearly twice Bitcoin’s losses and is looking at its worst year since 2022, co-founder Joseph Lubin is bullish over the future potential of Ethereum as an inflationary hedge.

Highlighting several key trends on-chain, he says that Ethereum has long-term potential to outperform both Bitcoin and gold as a store of value.

One of the main reasons for his argument is because of the DeFi ecosystem, stablecoins, and smart contracts on Ethereum. These allow ETH to have a stronger utility base compared to Bitcoin and gold.

In addition, with AI adoption accelerating, Ethereum could also see rising demand for its network infrastructure.

Ethereum’s inflation-hedge narrative faces a key test.

As previously mentioned, the U.S. market is facing a range of risks. This has made this one of the most volatile times for investors. So far, Ethereum has had trouble attracting risk capital, falling almost 30% during Q4 compared to Bitcoin, which has fallen only 17%.

This puts Joseph Lubin’s thesis under pressure, as Bitcoin continues to lead the store-of-value narrative while Ethereum struggles to keep up.

Gold, meanwhile, is also losing steam. The metal is trading in a technical downtrend with losses of more than 5% so far in Q4, as it has failed to move above $4,000. The most recent fall below this level has caused a loss of around $480 billion in market value in just 10 hours.

That said, gold still has a clear “relative-strength” advantage over crypto.

As the chart above shows, the ETH/XAU ratio has lost more than 19% in Q4, demonstrating that Ethereum has failed to make any progress relative to gold. This becomes a key level to monitor. A breakout could result in better technical support for Lubin’s long-term thesis.

For now, Ethereum is struggling to outperform Bitcoin and gold currently. Despite rising staking participation and stronger network security, ETH is yet to translate these fundamentals into stronger capital inflows or relative price performance.

Ethereum is showing growing network participation, supporting Lubin’s store-of-value thesis.

Despite this, ETH continues to lag Bitcoin and gold, with the ETH/XAU ratio down over 19% in Q4.

#5182. Nvidia Erases 2026 Gains as Republicans Threaten Jensen Huang With Subpoena

Nvidia Erases 2026 Gains as Republicans Threaten Jensen Huang With Subpoena

Nvidia shares plunged to $166.95 Tuesday afternoon, leaving the AI chipmaker down 18.42% for November and wiping out all the gains it had accumulated in 2026.

The selloff accelerated as congressional Republicans threatened to subpoena CEO Jensen Huang over his reported relationship with a suspected Chinese intelligence operative.

At that price, Nvidia was roughly 10.5% below its $186.50 closing price at the end of 2025 and 31.4% below its recent $243.37 peak. November is shaping up as its worst month since September 2022, when shares lost roughly 19.6%; the company suffered an even deeper 32% crash in April of that year.

Tuesday's drop exceeded 6% in afternoon trading, after a 4.3% fall on Monday. The two-day decline was on track to erase more than $470 billion in market value. Even after the significant drop, some analysts would still view the stock as extremely overbought in the long term, with prices much higher than what would be considered safe based on technical indicators.

Just for context, even an additional 20% crash (-43% from October) would be considered healthy in an isolated technical analysis, leaving the price still above the average price of the last 50 months. Prices tend to either bounce or trade near that line. When prices go too far away from the average, that usually means there is an unusual appetite for the asset, typically driven more by FOMO than fundamentals.

House Foreign Affairs Committee Chairman Brian Mast told ABC News on Tuesday that he was prepared to subpoena Huang unless the CEO responded by Wednesday, November 11, to a request for a closed-door briefing. No subpoena had been issued at the time of the report.

Mast and two other Republican committee chairs asked Huang to appear on November 18 and explain his interactions with Alice Huang, a former executive at Singapore-based chip buyer Megaspeed. The lawmakers also want details about Nvidia's safeguards against foreign espionage, according to a letter reviewed by Reuters.

U.S. authorities have reportedly investigated whether Alice Huang, who has no family relationship to the Nvidia CEO, passed information to Beijing. The allegations have not established that Jensen Huang knowingly disclosed confidential information or violated export rules.

On Sunday, Nvidia's board announced an independent investigation into whether its chief executive was targeted by a foreign intelligence operation. The board said it had no reason to believe that national security or company secrets were compromised, but would examine the available information.

Earlier today, the Financial Times reported Nvidia has hired former U.S. prosecutor Joon H Kim to conduct the internal investigation.

The company is also facing legal pressure. Shareholders filed a proposed class-action lawsuit Monday alleging that Nvidia and Huang failed to disclose risks associated with the reported relationship.

The scandal has landed during a broader reassessment of the AI boom. Investors have questioned how long massive spending on data centers can continue, and Nvidia's competitors are offering customers more alternatives to its expensive chips.

Google introduced new AI chip configurations Monday. The Information reported that OpenAI was considering buying Google's tensor processing units, or TPUs, which are processors built specifically to run AI workloads. Neither company has confirmed a purchase but yesterday Google announced a new line of TPUs available for select customers, directly competing against Nvidia’s chips.

Nvidia is scheduled to report quarterly earnings on November 17, one day before the congressional briefing requested by House Republicans. Mast's deadline for Huang to respond comes first, on November 11.

#5181. Bitcoin and Ethereum sink to 3-month lows, but a bigger crypto selloff may be ahead

A $727 million liquidation wave has exposed the market's fragility, with Bitcoin facing a critical $68,000 support test and deeper historical downside risks.

Bitcoin and Ethereum fell to three-month lows Thursday, deepening a cryptocurrency selloff that has erased $550 billion in market value since October.

Bitcoin slumped to $71,317, its lowest level since August, while Ethereum briefly fell below $2,000 to touch $1,983. The declines have pushed the broader crypto market's valuation from approximately $2.94 trillion in early October to $2.39 trillion.

The downturn has coincided with a sharp deterioration in market sentiment. CryptoQuant's Analyst Consensus Index fell from -1.0 to -1.8 standard deviations over seven days, signaling increasingly bearish expectations as investors grapple with persistent selling pressure and uncertainty over the market's next direction.

The latest price decline triggered widespread losses across cryptocurrency derivatives markets as leveraged traders were caught on the wrong side of the downturn.

According to CoinGlass, 125,731 traders were liquidated over 24 hours, with closed positions totaling about $727 million.

Bitcoin accounted for $293.8 million of those liquidations, with bullish positions representing 91.1% of the total. Ethereum followed with $120.2 million, of which 87.7% came from traders betting on higher prices.

Together, Bitcoin and Ethereum accounted for approximately $414 million, or 57% of the market-wide total.

Bitcoin and Ethereum accounted for about $414 million of the roughly $727 million market total, according to a rolling 24-hour CoinGlass snapshot on Nov. 12.

The selling intensified during a four-hour period when nearly $489 million in leveraged positions were liquidated. Long positions accounted for approximately $446 million of those losses, compared with $44 million in short positions.

The largest individual liquidation was a $31 million Bitcoin position on decentralized derivatives exchange Hyperliquid.

The concentration of losses among bullish traders illustrates how the price decline forced exchanges to close leveraged positions, adding to selling pressure as the market struggled to find support.

The downturn comes during weakening institutional demand and contracting liquidity across the crypto market.

Data from SoSoValue showed that US spot Bitcoin exchange-traded funds have recorded approximately $2 billion in net outflows over the past three weeks. Ethereum-focused products are also heading toward a seventh consecutive week of withdrawals, with more than $2.1 billion leaving the funds during that period.

The sustained withdrawals have coincided with a broader contraction in available crypto liquidity.

The supply of major dollar-linked stablecoins contracted by $14.2 billion over 30 days, while Bitcoin's apparent demand declined by 284,000 BTC.

These developments suggest that the latest liquidation wave struck a market already struggling to attract fresh capital, leaving fewer sources of demand to absorb the selling pressure.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Beyond the immediate selling pressure, Bitcoin's deteriorating technical structure and historical valuation indicators point to the market could face further losses before establishing a durable bottom.

The cryptocurrency has fallen below its 200-day moving average, which stood near $73,200, breaking a widely followed trend indicator used to assess the broader direction of prices.

The breakdown has shifted attention toward lower price levels where traders expect buying interest to emerge.

According to CoinGlass, Bitcoin's liquidation heatmap identifies approximately $68,000 as the next potential support region. A decline toward that level would extend the current downturn and test whether buyers can absorb additional selling pressure.

However, historical on-chain valuation data shows that Bitcoin could face a considerably deeper correction if market conditions continue to deteriorate.

Joao Wedson, chief executive of blockchain analytics firm Alphractal, highlighted Bitcoin's Cumulative Value Days Destroyed (CVDD) indicator, which currently places a historical bear-market valuation reference near $52,700.

The metric uses the value and age of Bitcoin moving on-chain to identify price regions associated with previous market bottoms.

At a Bitcoin price of $72,500, the cryptocurrency was trading approximately 37.7% above the CVDD level. A complete retest would imply a further decline of roughly 27% from that reference price.

The $72,500 comparison price puts CoinGlass's estimated $68,000 liquidation heatmap region about 6.2% lower and Joao Wedson's $52,700 CVDD reading about 27.3% lower.

Source: CoinGlass liquidation heatmap · Alphractal Academy (CVDD methodology)

Wedson pointed to previous market cycles in which Bitcoin approached or briefly traded below the indicator.

In December 2018, Bitcoin bottomed near $3,122 against a CVDD reading of $3,060. Similarly, the November 2022 market bottom occurred around $15,479, slightly below the indicator's $15,900 valuation.

Comparable patterns emerged during the January 2015 and March 2020 downturns, when Bitcoin traded below the metric before eventually recovering.

Still, the historical relationship does not establish that Bitcoin must revisit $52,700 during the current downturn.

Unlike a fixed technical support level, CVDD changes with on-chain activity as older Bitcoin moves between wallets. Its valuation threshold can rise over time, potentially narrowing the gap between the indicator and market prices without requiring an equivalent decline in Bitcoin.

That leaves the market facing two distinct price tests: whether buyers can defend the $68,000 region identified by CoinGlass, and whether a sustained recovery can push Bitcoin back above its 200-day moving average.

A failure to stabilize around those levels would strengthen concerns that the latest selloff is developing into a more prolonged correction, bringing the deeper historical valuation levels highlighted by Wedson into focus.

#5180. 'Yes': SBI CEO Yoshitaka Kitao Answers XRP Holder Doubts in One Word

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Yoshitaka Kitao, head of Japanese financial group SBI Holdings, answered "YES" when asked point-blank whether he still trusts XRP.

The question came from an X user. The user wrote that the past few weeks had been "truly exhausting" for XRP holders, and said that token holders feel that Ripple has left them behind.

Kitao answered in English. Most of his recent posts on X are in Japanese, and the rest of them are reposts of other accounts.

Kitao previously worked as an executive at Nomura Securities and later served as CFO of SoftBank. In 1999, he founded SoftBank Investment, which later became SBI Holdings. Today, SBI includes banks, brokerage, and insurance businesses, as well as crypto services.

The partnership began in 2016. SBI joined Ripple's Series B round and launched a joint venture with Ripple, SBI Ripple Asia. SBI owns about 60% of the venture, and Ripple owns the remaining 40%.

The same year, SBI Ripple Asia set up a consortium of Japanese banks to test Ripple's technology for payments, and the consortium later grew to more than 60 banks.

In 2018, SBI launched the interbank app MoneyTap. The same year, SBI's crypto exchange, now called SBI VC Trade, started operations with XRP trading. In 2019, Kitao joined Ripple's board of directors.

From 2020, SBI began handing the token out to shareholders as bonuses, and the payment service SBI Remit launched remittances using it with on-demand liquidity (ODL), including in the major Japan-Philippines corridor.

SBI kept working with Ripple during the SEC lawsuit against the company, which lasted from December 2020 until 2025.

Back on February 15, 2026, in rare long-form tweets in English, Kitao denied rumors that SBI holds $10 billion worth of XRP: "Not $10 billion in XRP, but around 9% of Ripple Labs. So our hidden asset could be much bigger." By his account, the combined valuation of Ripple Labs and its ecosystem is "enormous," and SBI owns more than 9% of all of it.

Kitao also talked about XRP at SBI's quarterly briefing on November 2, 2026. He said that investors buy Bitcoin mostly to hold it, and that the value of XRP depends on its use in payments and business ecosystems.

In August 2025, SBI VC Trade and Ripple agreed to bring the RLUSD stablecoin to Japan. In October 2025, SBI also invested $200 million in Evernorth, a company that holds XRP in its treasury. By now, SBI VC Trade has more than two million accounts.

#5179. Everyone’s selling XRP right now, but why?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

XRP [XRP] has been sliding on the charts for weeks, and it looks like everyone’s selling their stash right now.

Whales have been steady though. They’ve been trading big, but we don’t quite know whose side they’re on.

Cryptoquant’s Futures Taker CVD has been sell-dominant since late August. That hasn’t changed yet so, two and a half months straight.

The only time this year Futures traders leaned buy-heavy was in the beginning of 2026. In that time, XRP has gone from $1.55 in late September to $1.05 now.

Spot traders used to turn a blind eye, but not anymore. For the most part, their CVD was neutral this year. There were some buy-heavy stretches in January, February and May.

That changed in October. Now, the Spot Take CVD is also sell-dominant. This is the first time that’s happened all year!

That matters more than you think. Futures selling might just be traders betting short. When Spot starts selling too, that would mean that people holding actual XRP may be getting out.

Usually, when people trade a coin this much, it’s because the price is rising. With XRP though, that’s not been the case.

Futures volume bubble map went red in late August. Through most of September and early October, it was marked as “overheating.” While that’s slowed down a bit since, it’s still been a fair spike.

It moved into “heating” in early September, was “overheating” in the middle of the month, and has stayed hot ever since.

Now, put that next to when Spot volume was “cooling” at $1.40.

Both markets are busy right now, but since it’s all during a dip, it may be indicative of selling.

Finally, CryptoQuant’s Average Order Size has shown “big whale orders” on Futures and Spot since early October.

That’s happened before as well. Whale-sized orders were all the rage through June, July, and most of August. All this, while XRP went between $1 and $1.25.

Towards the end of August, the price went back to $1.50 on the charts.

As with many others, this metric also only shows how big orders are, and not which way they’re going. Whales could be buying the dip, or walking away.

Either way, they’re the ones moving the most money right now. Whatever they decide at $1 is probably where XRP goes next.

#5177. Trump Media Posts $410 Million Profit. Bitcoin Gains Did the Heavy Lifting

Trump Media Posts $410 Million Profit. Bitcoin Gains Did the Heavy Lifting

Trump Media & Technology Group reported a $409.7 million third-quarter profit on Monday, a sharp turnaround for the company behind President Donald Trump's Truth Social.

However, most of the money that Trump Media & Technology made didn't come from media or technology. It came from the rising value of its crypto holdings and related investments.

The company generated just $2.97 million in revenue during the three months ended September 30, according to its SEC filing. A year earlier, it had reported a $54.8 million loss.

Trump Media recorded $496.1 million in realized and unrealized gains on digital assets, pledged crypto and equity securities, according to its earnings release. Much of that was an accounting gain—assets rose in value even when the company hadn't sold them for cash.

Digital assets and pledged coins accounted for $378.1 million of those gains, with securities adding another $118 million. Derivative losses and other expenses offset part of the windfall.

Bitcoin supplied a major boost. Chief Financial Officer Phillip Juhan said on the earnings call that it rose about 42% during the quarter, from roughly $59,000 at the end of June to $84,000 at the end of September.

Trump Media held approximately 15,649 Bitcoin at quarter-end, including around 11,385 coins pledged to a program designed to generate additional returns. Some of those assets are subject to arrangements allowing the counterparty to reuse the coins, according to the filing.

The company expanded into crypto in July 2025, purchasing about $2 billion in Bitcoin and related securities. That decision has tied its earnings closely to changes in digital-asset prices.

The downside was obvious just three months ago. Back in August Trump Media lost $238 million in the second quarter as declining crypto prices battered its holdings. This quarter, the accounting effect went in the opposite direction.

Truth Social's underlying business improved, though from a small base. Revenue increased 205% from about $973,000 a year earlier, supported by subscriptions for Truth+ streaming and Truth API, a paid service that gives customers faster access to posts on the platform that critics have likened to insider trading.

The company also recorded growth in advertising sales and fees from its Truth.Fi funds. Even so, Trump Media reported a $12.3 million adjusted EBITDA loss, a measure that excludes investment gains and certain other items, compared with a $29.1 million loss a year earlier.

The latest quarterly profit has not repaired the longer-term picture for shareholders. DJT shares traded around $4.20 Tuesday, up about 3.3% on the day but down 94.7% from their $79.38 registered back in 2024. The stock has lost nearly 70% this year.

Trump Media still posted a $234.2 million net loss for the first nine months of 2026. It is also pursuing a $6 billion merger with fusion-energy developer TAE Technologies, which shareholders are scheduled to vote on December 14.

Before that vote, holders of the company's $1 billion in convertible notes can demand cash repayment on November 30, according to its quarterly filing.

#5176. Ripple's RLUSD Overtakes PayPal's PYUSD

Ripple's US dollar-backed stablecoin RLUSD has set a new record by surpassing PayPal USD (PYUSD) in market capitalization to cement its position as a top stablecoin globally.

Recent data from CoinGecko shows that RLUSD's market cap has grown to around $2.71 billion, enabling it to surpass its competitor, PayPal's stablecoin, with a market cap of around $2.69 billion.

This milestone was achieved less than two years after Ripple's formal introduction of RLUSD in December 2024, underscoring its rapid growth in the crypto market.

A prominent XRP Ledger validator, Vet, also commented on this development to underscore the increasing significance of RLUSD in the stablecoin industry.

"RLUSD is now a Top 5 USD backed stablecoin. On its way to be in Top 3," Vet commented.

Vet noted Ripple's long-standing contribution to building the XRP Ledger. He added that the increasing adoption of RLUSD could potentially lead to faster adoption of the blockchain by traditional financial institutions.

Vet stressed the increasing relevance of RLUSD's rising payment volume and increased use of the stablecoin on the XRP Ledger.

He also noted that RLUSD has opened the door to numerous integrations with banks and crypto exchanges.

The XRP Ledger validator further noted that these integrations could motivate financial institutions to not only run infrastructure to support the XRPL but also integrate other tokenized assets on it.

This new record for RLUSD comes on the back of several key institutional partnerships and exchange integrations for RLUSD that Ripple has been announcing recently.

On Oct. 28, Ripple announced a partnership with Coinbase to provide RLUSD for retail and institutional users across Coinbase Exchange, Coinbase Prime and its retail trading app.

As part of the partnership, Coinbase also announced support for RLUSD across different blockchain networks such as the XRP Ledger, Ethereum and Base.

On the other hand, Ripple's partnership with banking giant BNY, which is the custodian of reserves for RLUSD, is also deepening.

Besides this, Ripple has also managed to secure support from Standard Chartered, which launched institutional custody services for RLUSD on the XRP Ledger back in October.

#5175. Helius encrypts Solana transfers without removing operator trust

Its documented default prover, the service that constructs zero-knowledge proofs, adds a hardware requirement where secret transaction inputs are processed inside an attested AWS Nitro enclave, an isolated computing environment.

Solana can check that a transfer satisfies the protocol’s rules, but protecting the inputs while that proof is made depends on the prover’s isolation, code, and deployment policies.

The practical question for wallet developers integrating the launch is which of those protections their software can verify.

Helius’s wallet integration guide says proof generation needs plaintext inputs covering the complete transaction data and nullifier keys. It identifies an attested AWS Nitro enclave as the SDKs’ default prover.

The protocol specification lists private inputs including the asset, amount, blinding values, and nullifier secret for notes being spent, along with paths proving those notes belong to the state tree. The proof also includes output assets, amounts, and blinding values.

Those inputs become readable to the proving code inside the enclave, and the documented client protects them from the host operator by encrypting requests to a key held by the attested enclave.

The nullifier secret also derives the markers used to prevent repeat spending. In the native wallet integration, the wallet retains the signing key, and the TypeScript SDK leaves signing of the finished Solana transaction with the application’s signer.

Helius documents local decryption, which keeps the viewing key on the device, and delegated decryption, which shares a viewing key with an authorized provider. Delegation gives that provider read access to balances and history, without granting spending authority.

AWS describes Nitro Enclaves as isolated environments whose memory the parent instance’s processes or administrators cannot access. Enclaves have no persistent storage, interactive access, or external networking.

The tagged SDK refuses a remote prover request without one, before making an HTTP call. A wallet can pin a reviewed release policy or use a network policy authenticated by the SDK’s pinned signers. Signed policies refresh during attestation and expire after use.
