Helius’s documented default path sends encrypted proof inputs into an AWS Nitro enclave, then returns the proof to the wallet before transaction signing.

The client verifies attestation against an approved policy before encrypting remote proof requests.

Code review, AWS isolation and the operator’s initial KMS policy binding underpin the documented confidentiality design.

Wallets check an approved enclave and key; code review and deployment policy remain separate dependencies.

Documented default configuration only, not independent live attestation or an audit. Other proving or authorization paths can differ. Native wallet signing, remote proving and balance-viewing access have separate roles.

Qualitative data-flow diagram of the documented native-wallet default prover. No numerical scale or calculated values. The selectable panel distinguishes client attestation checks from code, hardware and deployment trust dependencies.

Documented default configuration only, not independent live attestation or an audit. Other proving or authorization paths can differ. Native wallet signing, remote proving and balance-viewing access have separate roles.

A wallet can automatically check that the enclave matches an approved software image and encryption key, yet deciding which image to approve still requires confidence in the code and its reviewers.

The Nitro deployment guide says the measured image includes the prover, startup script, certificate bundle, allowed external hosts, indexer URL, and KMS key identity. Its design uses AWS Key Management Service to release the shared encryption seed only to matching attested enclaves.

The guide says clients cannot read the KMS key policy and rely on the operator’s deployment for that guarantee. It identifies AWS KMS, AWS Support, Nitro hardware, and the operator as trust roots during initial policy binding.

Even correctly encrypted requests leave metadata visible to the parent, such as paths, queries, timing, and sizes. The path reveals the proof’s circuit shape.

The versioned TEE guide records a mainnet build based on v0.6.0-alpha, with measured-build provenance, distinct from the ts-sdk-v0.8.0-alpha documentation tag.

The specification allows client-side proofs, and the repository supplies localnet tooling and downloadable prover binaries.

Helius’s launch announcement says Certora, zkSecurity, and OtterSec audited programs and circuits. Its security page lists Certora in October and zkSecurity in August, while marking OtterSec as awaiting a report.

The launch gives wallet developers a trust decision on which code and deployment policy they approve to handle proof inputs.

Velina Tchakarova, geopolitical strategist and founder of FACE (For A Conscious Experience), argues that we’re in a new Cold War and that the US and China are building two rival financial architectures. #5173. XRP, ETH, DOGE: Tim Draper Predicts Bitcoin to Absorb Altcoins

Billionaire venture capitalist and longtime Bitcoin advocate Tim Draper has made a bold prediction about the future of the cryptocurrency industry, suggesting that Bitcoin (BTC) will eventually absorb innovations developed by competing altcoins.

His comments have significant implications for major cryptocurrencies such as XRP, Ethereum (ETH) and Dogecoin (DOGE), which have established their own ecosystems and compete for market share in the broader cryptocurrency industry.

In a recent post on X, Draper compared Bitcoin's growing dominance to the rise of Microsoft, which managed to establish itself as a leading software company by incorporating successful innovations from competing businesses.

"Microsoft never invented the spreadsheet, the word processor, or the slide deck. It absorbed them," Draper stated.

The billionaire investor pointed to the success of Lotus 1-2-3 and WordPerfect, two pioneering software applications that eventually lost their dominant positions to Microsoft's Excel and Word.

Draper believes that a similar transformation is taking place in the cryptocurrency industry, with Bitcoin potentially emerging as the ultimate beneficiary of innovations pioneered by alternative blockchain networks.

"Altcoins are the Lotus 1-2-3s and WordPerfects of this era," he wrote.

According to Draper, smaller cryptocurrencies essentially serve as testing grounds for technological innovations that could eventually become integrated into Bitcoin's expanding ecosystem.

"They prove an innovation works, and then that innovation with the strongest gravitational pull gets ported to the Bitcoin platform," he explained.

Draper also highlighted Bitcoin's increasing share of the overall cryptocurrency market as evidence supporting his prediction.

The billionaire investor recalled watching Bitcoin's market dominance climb from approximately 40% to 50% and eventually 61%.

"Bitcoin is that platform," Draper concluded, suggesting that the world's largest cryptocurrency could follow Microsoft's historical trajectory.

His argument raises questions about the long-term competitive positions of major altcoins, including Ethereum, XRP and Dogecoin.

Ethereum has established itself as a leading platform for smart contracts and decentralized applications, while XRP is primarily associated with cross-border payments and the broader XRP Ledger ecosystem. Dogecoin, meanwhile, remains one of the cryptocurrency industry's most recognizable meme coins.

Bitcoin's growing ecosystem of scaling and programmability solutions, including the Lightning Network and projects such as Stacks, nevertheless illustrates efforts to expand its capabilities beyond basic cryptocurrency transactions.

#5172. Bitcoin Miner HIVE Eyes $45 Million in Annual Revenue From AI Deal

Bitcoin Miner HIVE Eyes $45 Million in Annual Revenue From AI Deal

Bitcoin miner HIVE Digital Technologies is eyeing $45 million in annual revenue from an AI data center in Sweden, but it still hasn't secured a final lease.

In its latest earnings update, HIVE reiterated that its 32-megawatt Big Boden facility could generate the sum through a long-term colocation arrangement. That revenue depends on a definitive agreement, financing, facility upgrades, and the project entering service.

The company first disclosed the proposal in June. It involves an unnamed, investment-grade Swedish technology company and contemplates a lease lasting up to 10 years. During Tuesday's earnings call, CEO Aydin Kilic said the parties were negotiating deposits, termination rights, and other terms that could affect financing costs. A lender's consultant had visited the site the previous week, he said.

“We do not want to rush into a deal just for the sake of reporting new top-line revenue,” Kilic said, according to the earnings call transcript.

HIVE expects to seek financing for the Boden project after securing a signed lease, potentially through corporate bonds. The company has not announced a completion date for negotiations.

The existing data center would also need a substantial makeover. HIVE plans to retrofit it for up to 10,000 Nvidia GB300 GPUs, using liquid cooling to handle high-density computing racks.

Although the facility has a 32-megawatt utility connection, the proposed customer would receive approximately 25 megawatts of usable computing capacity, with the remaining power supporting data center operations.

“This site has evolved from a GPU compute facility supporting Ethereum,” Kilic said when announcing the letter of intent, describing its planned conversion into a more powerful AI installation.

The expansion follows HIVE's broader effort to move beyond Bitcoin mining, a shift that has already produced several large GPU-cloud contracts.

HIVE signed a major three-year AI computing agreement with Bell Canada and Cohere in June, covering 2,304 Nvidia GPUs as it expanded beyond crypto mining.

HIVE now reports $185 million in contracted GPU-cloud annualized run-rate revenue, although only $40 million is live. The remainder represents contracted capacity that has not yet entered service.

The company is targeting approximately $700 million in total high-performance computing annualized revenue by the end of 2028, though that figure includes projects requiring additional financing, construction, and customer commitments.

For Boden, HIVE's next milestone is securing a definitive lease before proceeding with project financing.

#5171. Crypto Exchange Gemini Sells U.K. and EU Units to KAST for Under $10 Million

Gemini Space Station has sold its already shuttered U.K. and European operations to Singapore stablecoin payments firm KAST for less than $10 million, according to CoinDesk reporting, as the exchange prepares to report third-quarter results after Thursday’s close.

Gemini declined to comment. A spokesperson for KAST would not discuss the transaction, but said the company is actively looking at acquisitions as its revenue and footprint grow. Neither firm has issued a public confirmation of a sale, and no regulator has announced a change-of-control approval.

The disposal, if confirmed, would close a retreat Gemini laid out in February. On Feb. 4, the Nasdaq-listed company, known as Gemini (GEMI), approved a plan to exit and wind down its operations in the United Kingdom, the European Union and other European jurisdictions, and Australia, as part of a broader effort to cut expenses and support a path to profitability. It said it would keep operating in the United States and Singapore.

The plan included a reduction in force of up to 200 employees in Europe, the United States and Singapore, or about 25% of Gemini’s global workforce as of that date. The company estimated about $11 million in pre-tax restructuring charges, mostly in cash, and said it expected the wind-down to be substantially completed in the first half of 2026. Reuters reported the job cuts the same day.

Co-founders Cameron and Tyler Winklevoss, who started Gemini in 2014 as a bitcoin exchange, told customers the firm would “double down on America” after foreign markets proved hard to win and stretched the company’s cost base. Gemini later told U.K., European Economic Area and Australian customers that their accounts would close on April 6, with withdrawals-only starting in early March.

What would change hands in a sale is the leftover entities, staff and regulatory permissions from those already wound-down units, not Gemini’s core U.S. exchange. Buyers of shuttered crypto businesses often want the licenses more than the remaining operations. Any transfer of a licensed firm in the U.K. or the EU typically still requires a change-of-control review by local regulators before a new owner can run it.

KAST is a Singapore-based financial platform built on stablecoin rails. In March, it said it had raised $80 million in a Series A round co-led by QED Investors and Left Lane Capital. The company, founded in July 2024 by former Circle executive Raagulan Pathy, said it would use the capital for expansion, licensing and compliance.

KAST provides dollar-denominated accounts, cross-border payments and cards that let consumers and businesses hold, move and spend stablecoins. In the March release, it said it had more than one million users and was processing nearly $5 billion in annualized transaction volume. Those figures have not been independently verified, and KAST has not publicly described a European acquisition.

The report lands on a busy day for Gemini. The company said on Oct. 20 that it would release third-quarter results after the market close on Thursday, Nov. 12, with a conference call at 8:30 a.m. ET on Friday. Investors will be looking for evidence that the February restructuring is lowering costs and moving Gemini closer to the profitability it set as a goal when it pulled back overseas.

The Winklevoss twins earlier this year put $100 million of bitcoin into the company after the stock came under pressure. Thursday’s results will show how the business looks after that overseas wind-down.

A sale of the U.K. and EU units for less than $10 million would be a modest figure next to the $11 million in restructuring charges Gemini already booked around the exit. The larger question is whether a buyer can put those European permissions to work, and whether Gemini confirms the deal when it reports Thursday evening.

#5170. Prisoner Jailed for 8 Years Over $2.7M Crypto Theft Plot Run From His Cell

Prisoner Jailed for 8 Years Over $2.7M Crypto Theft Plot Run From His Cell

A man who directed an attempted £2 million ($2.7 million) cryptocurrency theft from his prison cell has been jailed for eight years by Scotland's High Court of Justiciary.

Robert Barr, 29, pleaded guilty along with Sean Favier, 26, and Iain Doyle, 37, to being involved in serious organised crime as part of the attempted theft, according to a sentencing statement published by Scotland's Judicial Office on Tuesday. Lord Cubie sentenced Favier to four years and Doyle to five years at the High Court in Glasgow.

"This was a crime which married 21st century computer sophistication with old fashioned housebreaking in an audacious attempt to steal cryptocurrency worth up to £2 million pounds," Lord Cubie said.

The plan, according to the court's account, was for Favier and Doyle to get into the victims' home in Midlothian, near Edinburgh, and make the man go onto his computer and download AnyDesk, a remote-access program. That would let Barr, who was serving a 21-month sentence at HMP Edinburgh for fraud, take control of the computer from prison and transfer the crypto to wallets he controlled.

The two men went to the house at around 11:30 p.m. on April 17, 2024, but the couple saw them on their doorbell camera and refused to let them in. One of the men looked into the lens and asked whether there was a camera in front of him before the pair left, the BBC reported.

The attempt followed months of preparation, including identifying the victim as a crypto holder, gathering images of the house and reconnaissance, all of which investigators pieced together from Barr's phone after it was seized in June 2024, the judge said. Messages on the phone "plainly contemplated threats and/or violence," he added.

The victim was a co-founder of a crypto startup who did not advertise his holdings, but followed mostly crypto-related accounts on social media, according to the BBC. Barr's associates had already burgled the house in January 2024 and taken a crypto wallet, but could not access the funds without its PIN, the court heard.

Prosecutors described Barr as a "sophisticated cybercriminal" able to "orchestrate a level of criminality rarely seen within mainstream investigations." He had a previous conviction for a crypto fraud, and organised the new offence while in prison "using plainly illicit means of being in contact or online," Lord Cubie said, calling those materially aggravating factors.

The judge said that the plot was only frustrated because the victims "had the sense not to open their front door" to unknown men with their faces covered. Lord Cubie will consider a serious crime prevention order against Barr on November 17. Barr is also in custody awaiting potential extradition, the judge noted.

Had the men got inside, the plot would likely have escalated into a so-called wrench attack, in which criminals use force or the threat of it to make crypto holders hand over their funds. Such attacks are on pace for a record year, with more than $30 million stolen in the first half of 2026, according to a Chainalysis report.

The UK has seen several recent cases. Three teenagers were jailed earlier this year after stealing $4.3 million in crypto at knifepoint, and three men were sentenced last month over a failed home invasion aimed at stealing crypto. In September, Crimestoppers offered a reward of up to £10,000 for information on an attack on a UK couple in which, police believe, a fourth man relayed instructions to the three attackers over a video call.

#5169. Anthropic’s new AI leads freelance benchmarks, yet fails 58% of the tasks

Anthropic’s Fable 5.2 tops a freelance work benchmark at 41.3%, meeting professional standards on roughly two in five projects.

Anthropic’s Fable 5.2 ranks first on the Remote Labor Index, a test of complete freelance projects, with 41.3% of its deliverables judged to meet a professional reference standard.

Scale’s Nov. 12 leaderboard lists Fable 5.2 at 41.3%, ahead of the next-highest model, Opus 5.5 at 21.25%. Anthropic reported the result in its Nov. 9 release announcement, labeling the RLI score “High effort” and indicating that safeguards were enabled.

The original RLI paper, submitted in October 2025 by researchers from the Center for AI Safety and Scale AI, describes a dataset of 240 projects across 23 freelance work categories. Each includes a brief, the files needed to complete the task, and a professional human-reference deliverable.

The selected projects include documents, audio, video, 3D models, and computer-aided design files. That range makes file production and finished-product quality part of the challenge, alongside understanding what the client requested.

The researchers’ original evaluation method uses trained human reviewers to inspect the AI’s work against the brief and reference deliverable.

Independent evaluations and majority voting determine outcomes, and a project passes when the AI’s deliverable satisfies the brief at least as well as the reference, at a quality a reasonable client would accept.

A useful prototype or a partial result can therefore fall below the pass threshold, even when it demonstrates relevant skills. Passing demands a deliverable that clears the professional reference standard.

#5166. JPMorgan Lifts Its Strategy (MSTR) Stake 36% and Holds $540 Million in Bitcoin ETFs

JPMorgan Chase raised its Strategy (MSTR) stake 36% in the third quarter, a Form 13F filed Thursday shows, lifting the position to 848,932 shares worth $131.3 million at Sept. 30, weeks before an MSCI index exit.

That is up 224,545 shares from the 624,387 JPMorgan reported at June 30, when the same holding was valued at $57.87 million. Part of the dollar jump is the stock. Strategy’s implied price on the two filings rose from $92.68 at June 30 to $154.67 at Sept. 30, so the disclosed stake more than doubled in value even though the share count rose 36%.

The same filing shows a much larger bitcoin ETF line. JPMorgan held 11,408,930 shares of BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust (IBIT) worth $539.98 million at Sept. 30, up 1,001,295 shares, or 9.6%, from 10,407,635 shares worth $355.73 million three months earlier. Rounded to the nearest million, that IBIT line is the $540 million bitcoin ETF stake.

Smaller positions in other spot funds added a little more. The bank also held 155,785 shares of Grayscale’s Bitcoin Mini Trust worth $5.76 million and 66,264 shares of the Bitwise Bitcoin ETF worth $3.00 million, plus much smaller lines in Fidelity’s Wise Origin Bitcoin Fund, Grayscale’s Bitcoin Trust and Invesco’s Galaxy Bitcoin ETF. Together, the spot bitcoin ETF shares came to about $549 million. Option positions are listed separately and are not part of that total.

JPMorgan filed a holdings report that folds in positions across 17 other included managers, so the Strategy and IBIT lines can include client, fund and discretionary positions rather than a single proprietary bet, and the firm may have changed those holdings since Sept. 30. The table lists 35,990 entries valued at $1.84 trillion. Executive Director Michael T. Lees signed the report in New York on Nov. 12.

The $540 million figure is shares only. JPMorgan also reported IBIT call options covering 3,703,200 shares, valued at $175.27 million, and put options covering 2,915,000 shares, valued at $137.97 million.

The Strategy options are larger still, relative to the common stock. The filing lists put options covering 1,148,500 Strategy shares, valued at $177.64 million, more than the $131.3 million stock line, and call options covering 349,500 shares, valued at $54.06 million. A 13F does not say whether those options are hedges, market-making inventory or client positions, and they should not be read as a single directional bet.

Other bitcoin-linked equities showed up as well. JPMorgan held 1,581,814 shares of Coinbase (COIN) worth $300.58 million, up 13% from June, and 128,788 shares of Strive (ASST) worth $3.77 million, a 179% increase in the share count. Bank of New York Mellon, in a 13F filed Nov. 5, raised its own Strive stake 28% to 237,100 shares.

The Sept. 30 snapshot is already dated in one important way. MSCI is removing Strategy from its MSCI USA Index after the close on Nov. 30, a step that also takes the stock out of the MSCI World and ACWI indexes. Strategy shares traded near $114.60 Thursday morning, down about 6% on the day. At that price, the 848,932 shares reported at quarter-end would be worth about $97 million, below the $131.3 million marked on the 13F.

Bitcoin traded near $71,300 Thursday morning, still below $72,000 after slipping through that level earlier in the session. That move is not reflected in the filing, which stops at Sept. 30.

Institutional managers that exercise investment discretion over $100 million or more in 13F securities have 45 days after quarter-end to disclose those holdings. That window ran to Nov. 14, a Saturday, so the deadline is Monday, Nov. 16. Wells Fargo, Millennium Management and Schonfeld Strategic Advisors had not appeared on the SEC’s EDGAR system as of Thursday morning.

The filing does not show what JPMorgan did after Sept. 30, as bitcoin fell and MSCI announced the Strategy deletion. The record it left is 848,932 Strategy shares, up 36%, and nearly $540 million in IBIT.

#5165. Avalanche Won Over the NYSE. Now It Has to ‘Capture Value’ for AVAX

Avalanche Won Over the NYSE. Now It Has to ‘Capture Value’ for AVAX

The New York Stock Exchange this week selected Avalanche to provide infrastructure for a tokenized securities platform it's prepping. Experts say that it's a big deal for Avalanche's reputation, but warn that it may not translate into an uptick in value for the network's AVAX token.

On Monday, Ava Labs announced Avalanche would serve as the "core ledger technology" for a tokenized securities platform developed by NYSE parent company Intercontinental Exchange. The product will be designed to allow 24/7 trading, clearing, and settlement of tokenized securities, subject to regulatory approval.

Carlos Guzman, research analyst at market maker GSR, told Decrypt it’s “definitely a big win for the project.”

“Having NYSE choose its tech for settlement is a big institutional credibility boost, and it validates the benefits blockchain tech can provide to traditional financial plumbing with instant settlement and programmability,” he said.

Luther Maday, head of North America at crypto payments firm Mercuryo, told Decrypt that the decision came after a year of testing on Avalanche, which signals the network passed checks on "performance, reliability, and control."

"NYSE has chosen the technology for the clearing and settlement layer, which is a much deeper use of blockchain than simply issuing a tokenized asset on a network," Maday added.

Avalanche's AVAX token has risen 3.6% in the past day to $8.64, according to Decrypt data. But experts warn that providing infrastructure doesn't necessarily mean a long-term increase in token value.

"NYSE will be using Avalanche’s infrastructure for clearing and settlement, so the actual trading will still happen off-chain," Guzman explained.

This means NYSE activity will run on dedicated Avalanche infrastructure rather than the public C-Chain, where most of the network's activity happens.

"I would be careful about translating the news directly into expectations for activity on the public C-Chain," Maday said.

That said, Maday said to keep an eye on the details of how the AVAX token is used within the dedicated infrastructure. He also suggested that stablecoin liquidity, validator activity, and network fees could see a boost.

The NYSE has also teamed up with Blockchain.com to sell tokenized stocks and ETFs to crypto investors, while its parent company ICE is working with OKX on a 24/7 tokenized stock venue under an SEC exemption. Morgan Stanley, meanwhile, has started tokenizing securities of its own.

Not every company wants in, though. All seven of the so-called "Magnificent Seven" stocks—Apple, Meta, and the like—opted out of having their shares tokenized on the OKX-ICE venue, along with dozens of other companies.

Even if tokenization does take off, that doesn't guarantee profits for AVAX investors.

"The main question for Avalanche is whether it can do what other blockchains have struggled to do: capture value," Misha Putiatin, founder of collateral markets platform Symbiotic, told Decrypt. "Avalanche has always had an unusually outcome-focused team for this industry, so I suspect they have plans for that, but it remains to be seen."

Ava Labs did not immediately respond to Decrypt's request for comment.

#5162. Israel Discount Bank to Let Customers Trade Bitcoin in App Starting Nov. 26

Israel Discount Bank said Wednesday that from Nov. 26 it will let eligible customers buy, hold and sell bitcoin and ether in the bank’s mobile app, a launch that would beat larger rival Bank Leumi if it stays on schedule.

The Tel Aviv-listed lender described the service as a first for Israel’s banking system in a Nov. 11 statement. Customers will be able to trade the two assets alongside stocks and bonds, without opening an account at an outside exchange or managing a separate digital wallet.

The offer is limited to private, not corporate, customers who are Israeli residents, 18 or older, and whose accounts are already set up for securities trading, subject to the bank’s terms.

“For the first time in the banking system, Discount Bank will allow trading in digital currencies directly from the bank account in a simple and secure manner, without the need for complex dedicated digital wallets,” Avi Levi, CEO of Discount Group, said in the statement. “This is a first step toward establishing advanced technological infrastructure in the digital currency space and developing the bank’s capabilities in this area.”

The rollout was led by Elad Fisher, head of the bank’s financial markets division. Coins will sit in bank-provided wallets built on infrastructure from Fireblocks.

JUST IN: 🇮🇱 $150 billion Israel Discount Bank will become the 1st bank in Israel to let customers buy, hold and sell Bitcoin 👀Adoption! 👏 pic.twitter.com/gwKBy17cSf

The bank’s product page lists a 2% commission on purchases and sales in the app, not including any foreign-exchange conversion. Custody fees are waived for six months from the Nov. 26 launch and then run 0.25% a quarter. Trading is open Sunday through Friday from 9 a.m. to 11 p.m. Israel time, and only immediate orders are allowed. Customers can buy as little as 0.01 of a coin. Holdings appear in the securities portfolio at once, with cash settling the next business day in U.S. dollars; conversion into shekels happens only if the customer asks.

The service is a closed system for now. Bitcoin and ether bought through Discount cannot be sent to an outside wallet, and coins held elsewhere cannot be transferred in. The bank said it may revisit transfers later. It also warns of unusual risk, high volatility and the possibility of a total loss, and says the coins are not legal tender and lack the same protections as securities.

What Discount is selling, besides convenience, is tax treatment. Capital gains and losses on the coins can be offset automatically against the rest of the customer’s investment portfolio for tax withheld at source, the bank said, at a 25% rate on profits. Sale proceeds land directly in the customer’s bank account, without the source-of-funds questionnaires that have often accompanied money coming back from crypto platforms.

Discount is not the first Israeli bank to promise bitcoin trading. Bank Leumi, the country’s largest lender, announced a plan as early as March 2022 to offer bitcoin and ether through Pepper Invest with Paxos, a project that did not reach a full-scale launch. In August, Leumi and Galaxy Digital (GLXY) said Leumi and Pepper customers would be able to buy, hold and sell bitcoin, ether and Solana in the Leumi Trade app, with a launch expected in early 2027.

That announcement billed Leumi as the first bank in Israel to offer the service. If Discount opens on Nov. 26, it would get there first, though Leumi still plans to offer Solana, which Discount is not including at the start. Discount said it may later consider adding other assets.

In an Oct. 7 circular, the Bank of Israel’s Banking Supervision Department amended Directive 411 to say that a bank may not refuse payment services solely because the funds come from virtual-currency activity, a change that takes effect May 1, 2027. The amendment does not authorize banks to offer crypto trading, but it is part of a broader effort, first floated in a July draft, to widen how banks handle crypto-related payments.

The test now is whether Discount hits the Nov. 26 date, and whether customers accept a closed wallet and a 2% commission in exchange for bank custody and automatic tax withholding. Leumi still has early 2027 to catch up.

#5161. Solana treasury Forward Industries flashes a 30% SOL discount, with a catch

Forward Industries’ Solana treasury trades at a 29.7% NAV discount that narrows to 2.4% when potential share dilution is counted.

Forward Industries' Nov. 10 update puts the Solana treasury company's shares at a 29.7% discount to net assets using common shares alone.

Counting all the potential shares in the same disclosure, that discount shrinks to 2.4%. The difference is who could eventually share in the assets.

The share count is key almost as much as the treasury for investors comparing Nasdaq-listed FWDI with buying Solana directly.

The capitalization update uses a $5.53 share price and $600.2 million of net asset value (NAV). Forward's accompanying 8-K says the figures are preliminary, unaudited, and incomplete.

Dividing the same net assets among more potential shares produces three distinct comparisons:

Each discount is calculated as 1 minus the share price divided by NAV per share, using unrounded values.

The middle count already recognizes substantial claims beyond issued common stock: 12.27 million vested warrants with a displayed exercise price of $0.00, 689,010 options and 1.05 million unvested restricted stock units. It includes unvested options as well as vested ones.

Using the displayed prices for the two in-the-money option categories would add about $3.4 million if both groups vested and exercised for cash. With price, share count, and everything else fixed, that lifts the calculated discount to about 17.3%.

The largest addition between the middle and broadest counts is 13.38 million advisor and lead-investor warrants with a one-cent exercise price. Other additions include out-of-the-money options and warrants, plus 875,324 unvested performance stock units.

The accompanying prospectus to Forward's Nov. 9 filing describes three equal tranches requiring closing prices at 150%, 200% and 250% of the placement purchase price for 20 of 30 trading days after the relevant registration effectiveness.

Against the disclosed $18.50 purchase price, those trigger levels work out to $27.75, $37 and $46.25, subject to applicable adjustments.

If all 13.38 million warrants were exercised for cash at one cent, Forward would receive only about $134,000. The advisor warrant form and lead-investor warrant form also permit cashless exercise.

#5160. Nvidia Sheds $430B in Two Days as Jensen Huang Faces Calls to Resign

Nvidia Sheds $430B in Two Days as Jensen Huang Faces Calls to Resign

Nvidia has lost about $430 billion in market value in two days, as pressure grows in Washington over CEO Jensen Huang's ties to a businesswoman U.S. intelligence reportedly concluded was a Chinese spy.

Nvidia shares closed down 5.5% at $168.89 on Tuesday, following a 4.3% drop on Monday, according to Yahoo! Finance. That leaves the stock down about 9.5% since Friday's close, wiping out roughly $430 billion in market value, according to Yahoo! Finance data. The sell-off, which saw shares slide as much as 8% on Monday, has wiped out all of Nvidia's gains for 2026. The shares were up 3.8% at $175.23 in premarket trading early on Wednesday.

The independent members of Nvidia's board have retained Joon Kim, a former acting U.S. Attorney for the Southern District of New York, and his firm Wachtell, Lipton, Rosen & Katz to carry out the investigation it announced on Sunday, the company said on Tuesday.

A new statement from the independent members of the NVIDIA Board: https://t.co/uSFDhiohG8

— NVIDIA Newsroom (@nvidianewsroom) November 10, 2026

Sen. Kevin Cramer, a North Dakota Republican on the Senate Banking Committee, called on Huang to step down, telling Politico late Tuesday that the risk of extortion was too great. "The potential for extortion is so tremendous that something as sensitive as Nvidia chips, I just think he's got to go," Cramer said, adding that the board's internal investigation was "fine, but not nearly enough."

Hours earlier, former National Economic Council director Larry Kudlow had urged Huang to resign on his Fox Business show. "Step down, Mr. Huang, right now," Kudlow said, adding that he was "not here to indict" Huang but that he should step aside while the investigations take place.

Cramer's committee has jurisdiction over bills that would curb Nvidia chip sales to China, including the AI Overwatch Act and the Chip Security Act, which are being considered for inclusion in the annual defense spending bill, Politico reported.

Senate Democratic leader Chuck Schumer, meanwhile, accused President Donald Trump of failing to act after being briefed on the relationship. "What a dereliction of duty," Schumer said on the Senate floor on Tuesday, according to CNBC. "He's supposed to protect America, not China, not his friends."

Rep. John Moolenaar (R-MI), who chairs the House Select Committee on China, said he wanted to know whether Huang shared privileged information with Alice Huang and whether he was influenced to lobby against chip export restrictions. "If it becomes less of an investigation, more of damage control, that's not going to work," Moolenaar told CNBC. House Foreign Affairs Committee Chairman Brian Mast told ABC News he was prepared to subpoena Huang unless he responded by Wednesday to a request for a closed-door briefing on November 18, the day after Nvidia reports quarterly earnings.

Not everyone in Washington wants the scandal to dominate. Rep. Jay Obernolte (R-CA), co-chair of the House AI task force, told CNBC that a personal scandal should not "interfere" with the policy debate over chip exports to China.

Trump has said the FBI is looking into the matter, and that Huang told him he had no idea the woman was a spy.

Alice Huang, who also goes by Huang Le and is not related to Jensen Huang, worked in banking and investment management in China before becoming the controlling shareholder of Singapore-based Megaspeed in March 2023, according to a Reuters review of corporate records and filings.

Megaspeed, which Nvidia lists as a cloud partner, has drawn U.S. scrutiny over whether it helped Chinese firms obtain export-controlled Nvidia chips, but there has been no public finding that it broke the rules, Reuters reported.

China has denied the intelligence allegations, and Reuters said it could not independently verify them. George Chen, a partner at consultancy The Asia Group, told the news agency that Jensen Huang "may also be viewed as a potential victim" if he was targeted without knowing who she was.

#5156. Binance Says It Has Fully Covered the $6.34 Billion Hack With Company Funds

Binance said Thursday it has completed coverage of the $6.34 billion Oct. 29 hack from company funds, two weeks after the largest crypto theft on record, even as withdrawals continue to reopen in phases.

In a Thursday announcement, the exchange wrote that it “has completed coverage of assets impacted in the security incident” and published a new proof-of-reserves snapshot. It repeated the same message on X.

That wording matters. Coverage means Binance has replaced the affected tokens from its own books, not that it has recovered the stolen coins from the attackers. The exchange said it committed more than $6.34 billion in company funds and bought the affected tokens through market and over-the-counter channels, drawing on a partial deployment of its Secure Asset Fund for Users (SAFU), a portion of its futures insurance fund above the level required under its risk-management framework, and its corporate treasury.

Binance has also committed to replenishing SAFU to about $1 billion after that partial draw, a step it said would keep the fund fully resourced as an emergency user-protection reserve.

A very small number of tokens the exchange no longer lists were also affected. Binance said those holdings will be handled under its delisting process and that users who hold them will be covered.

Binance took a new proof-of-reserves snapshot at 00:00 UTC on Nov. 12, or 7 p.m. Eastern on Wednesday, covering 198 tokens. That is a sharp jump from the 10 tokens included in the Oct. 31 snapshot published in the days after the breach. The new figures are now posted on the exchange’s proof-of-reserves website.

The snapshot, Binance said, “provides an updated view of assets that are fully accounted for.” Users cannot yet check that claim themselves. The exchange said the full zero-knowledge proof will take about 40 to 50 hours to generate because of the computational workload. After that, users holding the affected tokens “will be able to independently verify that their assets are backed at a minimum of 1:1.”

Until the proof is ready, the 1:1 backing is Binance’s statement, not something customers can confirm on their own.

Thursday’s update does not reopen the platform all at once. Binance said it continues to reopen withdrawals in phases and will share further details once they are confirmed. The exchange first reopened withdrawals on Nov. 1, starting with bitcoin and then expanding to other networks, and has kept the restart on a phased timetable since then.

The announcement also does not say how much of the stolen assets have been retrieved, and it does not name an attacker. Covering customer balances leaves that recovery question unanswered. The theft, which Binance first detailed in a Nov. 3 incident report, began with a fake job interview and a poisoned developer tool and drained hot wallets on Oct. 29, in what remains the largest crypto hack on record.

The exchange had already pledged more than $6.34 billion to cover customer losses in the hours after the breach, reported coverage of 90% and then 95% as it reopened withdrawals, and later said it had replenished 98% of affected tokens, with the remainder in less liquid assets that took longer to buy. Thursday’s statement is the first time Binance has said that process is complete.

Binance also said security remains a priority and that work on its infrastructure is still underway. For customers, the immediate tests are simpler: whether the zero-knowledge proof, due in about two days, backs the new snapshot, and how quickly the rest of the withdrawal queues come back.

#5155. Kraken Parent Payward Brings 200 UK and EU Stocks Onchain as xStocks

Kraken Parent Payward Brings 200 UK and EU Stocks Onchain as xStocks

Payward, the parent company of crypto exchange Kraken, has tokenized more than 200 UK and European stocks through its xStocks platform, it announced on Wednesday.

The first batch covers some of the most actively traded companies listed in the UK and the European Union, with more to be added on a rolling basis. Like other xStocks, each token is backed 1:1 by the underlying share, which is bought and held by a regulated custodian for as long as the token trades, according to Payward.

Payward's @xStocksFi is expanding beyond US tokenized equities.

In partnership with @GTNglobal, more than 200 of the UK and EU’s largest equities are now tokenized by xStocks, including HSBC, Shell, LVMH and Airbus.

The first international launch, with more markets to follow.…

The tokens are available to trade on Kraken and through other platforms in the xStocks Alliance, and on blockchains where xStocks already trade, including Solana, Ethereum and TON. They are offered in more than 110 markets, but remain unavailable to investors in the UK.

"A portfolio should not have borders or trading hours," said Mark Greenberg, Payward's chief commercial officer. "Tokenizing every other international market is the next step."

The launch takes xStocks past 1,500 tokenized stocks, ETFs and IPOs. The platform holds more than $1.1 billion in assets, has processed over $45 billion in total transaction volume, and has more than 550,000 holders, Payward said.

The new tokens are the first delivered under Payward's partnership with GTN, a brokerage infrastructure firm that handles execution and custody of the underlying shares. When the two partnered in July, Payward said it planned to start with Hong Kong-listed stocks; hundreds of Hong Kong listings will now follow in the coming weeks, the company said.

The expansion also delivers on part of Payward's partnership with the London Stock Exchange, announced in September, under which Payward agreed to tokenize the 100 largest London-listed companies. Subject to regulatory approval, the exchange intends to list xStocks and begin trading them on LSE 24, its planned near-24-hour trading venue, in 2027.

Payward has been drawing interest from traditional exchanges. Nasdaq agreed to invest $100 million in the company at a $21 billion valuation in September, building on a March partnership to bring tokenized stocks to market in 2027, while Deutsche Börse bought a $200 million stake in April.

The company has also been expanding through acquisitions, agreeing to buy derivatives exchange Bitnomial for $550 million and Lithuania's Saldo Bank as part of a push into the EU.

#5152. Simon Dixon: What It Would Take for Me to Sell My Bitcoin for Gold

Simon Dixon, CEO and co-founder of BnkToTheFuture, tells BMTV there are only three things that would make him sell all of his Bitcoin and buy gold.

Simon Dixon, CEO and co-founder of BnkToTheFuture, tells BMTV there are only three things that would make him sell all of his Bitcoin and buy gold. Since 2011, he says Bitcoin has done three things incredibly well: you can earn and hold it in self-custody, spend it peer-to-peer without a government in the middle, and rely on a fixed supply of 21 million as a hedge against long-term debasement. The real battle, in his view, is not whether Bitcoin gets adopted — he says institutional adoption is on a trajectory we have never seen — but whether custodians, Wall Street-owned miners, and treasury companies end up centralizing the network.

#5149. Morning Minute: What Anthropic’s $1.4 Trillion IPO Means for Crypto

Morning Minute: What Anthropic’s $1.4 Trillion IPO Means for Crypto

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

💵 What Anthropic’s $1.4 Trillion IPO Means for Crypto

Several key new details for Anthropic’s pending IPO were released on Tuesday. The IPO price range was set at $70 to $78 a share, selling 825 million Class A shares to raise about $64.4 billion (at the top of the range). Pricing is expected November 18, with trading starting November 19 on Nasdaq under ANT. Roughly 20% of the deal is set aside for retail, which is about double what a book this size usually gives individual investors.

Notably on Hyperliquid, ANT is trading at $1.8T with $100M in daily trading volume and $200M in open interest (a big enough market to provide some signal). So perhaps traders are a bit more bullish on ANT price action than Wall Street.

Alongside the IPO sits a $26 billion private placement of non-voting Class N stock, bought at the IPO price by seven of Anthropic’s own compute suppliers. Google and Nvidia take about $10 billion each, AMD $2.5 billion, Broadcom, Micron and Microsoft about $1 billion apiece, TSMC $500 million. Those shares carry no lock-up. Combined with the IPO, Anthropic walks away with roughly $84.6 billion for general corporate purposes, mostly compute.

Regarding financials, Anthropic just reported revenue of $33.2 billion through nine months against $2.5 billion a year earlier and a $1.26 billion operating loss. It reported a $104 billion net loss, almost all of it a non-cash charge on convertible notes. And $900.9 billion in committed spending, with $70.7 billion due inside twelve months.

The liquidity drain thesis has been circulating since April, when CoinDesk laid out the case that mega-IPOs pull speculative money from the same pool that funds crypto. Retail allocations are the mechanism. A dollar earmarked for an IPO ticket is a dollar not in Bitcoin or memecoins.

We already ran this experiment. SpaceX listed June 12 at more than $2 trillion. Bitcoin fell 29% going into it, bottomed at $58,500 on June 30, and then rose 37% off that low. But notably, the Bitcoin bottom was effectively in on June 5 at $59k, a full week ahead of the SPCX IPO.

So the question is not whether an IPO pulls money out. It’s whether that already happened. Bitcoin fell roughly 15% from its early-October high through a $6.3 billion exchange hack, an AI capex selloff and a Democratic sweep.

Two things make this deal smaller than it looks for crypto. The retail slice is roughly $12.9 billion against SpaceX’s $22 billion, about 59% as much. And the $26 billion private placement never touches public market liquidity at all, because it comes from Anthropic’s vendors, who then get paid back in compute contracts.

Thus, if the SpaceX pattern repeats, and with the ~60% factor, we could expect BTC to drop another 5% or so into the Anthropic IPO (notably, that’s right where the 200-week moving average is). And we would expect the effective bottom to be in sometime in the next 1-2 days.

The good news is, the pain is mostly behind us, in that scenario. We will find out soon enough if IPO history repeats itself for crypto…

#5147. Deutsche Bank launches crypto custody for companies and institutions

Clients can store and transfer Bitcoin, Ether and select stablecoins through the bank, which manages their wallets and private keys.

Germany’s largest lender, Deutsche Bank, launched its crypto custody service for institutional and corporate clients on Wednesday, according to an announcement shared with Cointelegraph.

The bank also signed a memorandum of understanding with Deutsche Asset Management, its majority-owned asset manager, to explore collaboration on tokenized money market funds, crypto asset exchange-traded commodities and stablecoins.

Deutsche Bank announced plans to offer the service to institutional and corporate clients in Europe on Sept. 16, pending regulatory clearance. Deutsche Bank’s listing for crypto custody and transfer services in the European Union’s Markets in Crypto-Assets Regulation (MiCA) register is dated Oct. 12 and names Germany’s BaFin as its regulator.

The service initially supports Bitcoin (BTC), Ether (ETH) and select stablecoins, including Circle’s USDC (USDC) and EURC (EURC), as well as AllUnity EURAU.

Gerald Podobnik, co-head of Deutsche Bank’s corporate bank, told Reuters in October that the bank planned to launch in Germany before expanding to Asian financial centers in 2027.

Deutsche Bank partnered with crypto infrastructure provider Taurus in 2023 to develop custody services after applying for a digital asset custody license in Germany.

#5142. Decrypt Becomes First News Publisher to Offer Perps Trading, Powered by Solana's Phoenix

Decrypt Becomes First News Publisher to Offer Perps Trading, Powered by Solana's Phoenix

What happens when the place you read the news becomes the place you act on it?

The news that moves markets and the tools to trade them have long lived in separate places. Decrypt is bringing them together.

Decrypt is integrating perpetual futures trading, powered by Phoenix, a fully onchain perpetuals exchange on Solana, making it the first media platform to offer perps trading directly alongside its journalism.

Readers will be able to take long or short positions on crypto, stocks and commodities from the same place they follow the news that moves them, without switching apps, connecting external wallets or moving funds between platforms.

"Information influences decisions, shapes opinions and moves markets," said Ilan Hazan, co-founder and COO of Decrypt. "A reader might discover an opportunity through a publication, develop a view and then leave to act on it somewhere else. The publisher monetizes the impression, while someone else monetizes the transaction. It means the publisher captures only a small part of the economic activity that its information and distribution generates."

Rather than treating media and financial products as separate businesses, he said, Decrypt is bringing them together. "We're allowing readers to go from understanding what's happening in the markets to taking a position, without leaving Decrypt.”

"Partnering with Decrypt closes the gap between reading the news and acting on it," said Kabir Sethi, Head of Business Development at Phoenix. "With over 100 Phoenix perpetuals markets built directly into Decrypt, readers can take in a story, form a view, and trade it in the same place. We are excited to help play a part in transforming Decrypt into a true information exchange."

Perpetual futures, or perps, are contracts that let traders bet on an asset's price rising or falling without an expiry date, typically using leverage. Leverage magnifies both gains and losses, and positions can be liquidated quickly when markets move against them.

Phoenix offers markets in crypto, U.S. stocks, gold and pre-IPO companies around the clock, with up to 40x leverage and low fees, according to its website. It is developed by Ellipsis Labs, a team that has built exclusively on Solana and whose CEO, Eugene Chen, has described perps as having "crazy product market fit."

"These are very, very strong, high revenue businesses, probably some of the best in the industry," Chen said of perps exchanges in a podcast interview with Blockworks' Lightspeed earlier this year. Phoenix routes each trade at the moment it lands onchain, splitting it between its order book and market makers "based on what's going to give you the best price," he explained.

Perps trading runs through the Decrypt Money Account, a single self-custodial account on Solana designed to work without seed phrases, gas fees or bridging.

One balance now funds it all: prediction markets on Decrypt Predict, Jupiter-powered token swaps, Earn and, now, perpetual futures. Users can also send funds privately, after Decrypt added confidential transfers through Solana infrastructure firm Helius.

Imagine reading about a public company's earnings and taking a position on its stock, at any hour. Or following a breaking story about Bitcoin and going long or short in the same moment.

From information to execution, in one place.

Announced in September, The Information Exchange is Decrypt's vision for the next generation of financial media: a single destination bringing together news, intelligence, market data, probabilities, direct financial participation—with perps forming the latest building block in that infrastructure.

News moves markets. Now readers can move with it.

Perpetual futures are leveraged products and carry a high risk of loss. Availability may vary by region.

#5141. Peter Schiff on the Bitcoin Crash: “Get the Hell Out”

Challenged on why two or three down years would matter for Bitcoin when gold and the Nasdaq have had similar streaks before recoveries, Peter Schiff says the difference is the backdrop.

Challenged on why two or three down years would matter for Bitcoin when gold and the Nasdaq have had similar streaks before recoveries, Peter Schiff says the difference is the backdrop. When a market can’t go up on the best news it has ever had, he argues, that normally indicates that it’s going to go down. He calls Bitcoin “all hype,” with no intrinsic value, income, or dividends, and says much of the supply now sits with ETF investors and Bitcoin treasury companies rather than people who want to use it as money. All of that hot money, he warns, is going to exit, the market will go down faster than it went up, and he expects a massive washout. Even long-term bulls, he says, will have cheaper opportunities after leveraged speculators are cleared out, and he adds that this is not the time to be hodling.

#5138. OKXICE moves ahead with tokenized stocks as blue chips push back

The Nov. 23 debut puts a new SEC exemption to the test as Nasdaq pursues its own tokenized stock plans after warning that trading across platforms could fragment liquidity.

Crypto exchange OKX and New York Stock Exchange parent Intercontinental Exchange (ICE) are set to launch tokenized stock trading on Nov. 23, despite objections from 67 companies to having their shares listed and warnings from Nasdaq that trading across disconnected platforms could fragment liquidity.

The venture, known as OKXICE, will operate under the US Securities and Exchange Commission’s temporary Innovation Exemption and offer eligible US customers round-the-clock trading. Under the exemption, venues must give companies a chance to object before listing shares tokenized by an unaffiliated third party. JPMorgan Chase, McDonald’s, SpaceX and Coca-Cola were among the companies that objected.

Tokenized stocks are blockchain-based tokens that represent company shares or track their price. The rights they carry depend on how they are structured. The distributed value of tokenized stocks and ETFs reached $3.88 billion on Nov. 11, up 13.4% over 30 days.

In comments to Cointelegraph, former New York governor and OKXICE co-chair Andrew Cuomo said investors would have “the same rights, protections and ownership that come with the underlying shares.”

OKX expects the venue to list more than 70 assets, including 59 exchange-traded funds (ETFs) and tokenized shares of Coinbase, Circle, BitGo, Robinhood and Strategy. The lineup also includes Strategy’s preferred shares.

Related: Public companies object to OKX-ICE’s tokenized stock plan

The rollout comes amid concerns that trading the same shares across traditional exchanges and blockchain platforms could split liquidity and make prices less reliable.

In October, a study by the International Monetary Fund found that the tokenized stocks were about 1.5 times as volatile as the same stocks in traditional markets. Decentralized exchanges had the weakest liquidity and their prices strayed further from those in traditional markets than prices on centralized exchanges did.

The IMF also warned that combining trading and settlement on a blockchain could remove safeguards that help markets absorb shocks. As tokenized markets grow, greater use of leverage and closer links between markets could amplify fire sales, liquidity runs and financial contagion.

Nasdaq argued in a January filing that trading tokenized stocks on platforms outside traditional rails could fragment liquidity and make it harder for investors to get the best available price. Regular US stock trading runs from 9:30 a.m. to 4 p.m. Eastern time on weekdays, excluding market holidays, though some brokers offer premarket, after-hours and overnight trading.

Cuomo described the current market structure was “built for another era,” arguing that crypto markets had already shown that trading could run around the clock. “Now we are bringing that standard to equities, without asking investors to settle for synthetic exposure or give up their shareholder rights,” he said.

#5136. OKX-ICE Tokenized Stock Venue Sets Launch, Turning to Crypto Companies After Big Tech Opts Out

OKX-ICE Tokenized Stock Venue Sets Launch, Turning to Crypto Companies After Big Tech Opts Out

After weeks of rejections from corporate America, OKX and ICE’s tokenized stock venue has found its launch lineup: crypto’s own public companies.

OKXICE said Wednesday that its Tokenized Securities Venue will open Nov. 23, letting eligible U.S. customers trade tokenized securities around the clock in the OKX app.

More than 70 assets are expected to be listed, including securities from Coinbase, Circle, Robinhood, Strategy, BitGo, Securitize and Better.com, plus 59 ETFs from Direxion. Strategy will also list its preferred stocks.

That’s a very different roster from the one the venue started with. As of its Nov. 5 notice, 67 companies, including every member of the “Magnificent Seven” tech giants, had objected to having their shares traded on the platform. Coinbase, Robinhood and Strategy were notably absent from that list, and all three are now among the venue’s launch issuers.

“The SEC has created a real opening for American markets to lead the next era of finance,” said former New York Gov. Andrew Cuomo, who co-chairs OKXICE, a joint venture between OKX and NYSE owner Intercontinental Exchange.

Securitize will provide the tokenization infrastructure. Each token represents an entitlement backed one-for-one by a real U.S. share, carrying dividends, voting rights and other shareholder benefits, according to the announcement.

Trading will run through automated market maker pools built on Uniswap v4 on X Layer, OKX’s Ethereum layer-2 network, and customers will hold their tokens in self-custodial wallets.

Strategy CEO Phong Le called tokenization on the venue “a natural next step” for the company’s preferred stocks, such as STRC.

Access is limited to eligible U.S.-based OKX customers who activate a CeDeFi Wallet, pass identity verification and complete onboarding with OKXICE. The venue operates under the SEC’s innovation exemption, which the agency unveiled in September after the Clarity Act failed to advance in the Senate. Under the exemption, companies can keep third-party tokenized versions of their stock off a given venue by objecting within 30 days.

The launch caps a busy stretch for Wall Street tokenization. DTCC’s new tokenization service, which runs partly on the Canton Network, went live in late October, and Morgan Stanley said it was among the first banks to use it.

#5134. US data center restrictions could give Bitcoin miners an edge in AI, though challenges remain

As US voters push back against new data centers, Bitcoin miners already expanding into AI could benefit from their existing facilities.

Bitcoin miners with power already secured could have an edge in the race to build AI data centers after voters in several US communities backed limits on new development. The midterms could also bring closer scrutiny from Congress over who pays for the grid upgrades those facilities need, industry experts told Cointelegraph.

Voters in several states weighed in on data center development during the Nov. 3 US midterms, when local measures appeared on the same ballots as congressional races. Amid concerns about rising electricity costs, water use and the impact on nearby communities, voters favored limiting development in 40 of the 41 local contests tracked by Ballotpedia.

The restrictions include citywide bans, limits on larger facilities, temporary pauses on new projects and voter approval requirements for some developments.

The results come as major Bitcoin mining companies continue moving into AI, a transition Jamie Brown, CEO of Global Kiwi Labs, said could become more attractive as local restrictions make it harder to build new data centers.

“If a Bitcoin miner has not yet repurposed their electric contract and physical plant to AI, the case to do so just got more compelling,” said Brown, whose company hosts servers for crypto exchanges and trading firms.

Related: US midterm elections 2026 as it happened: Democrats win control of Congress

Miners’ shift to AI infrastructure is already underway, with publicly listed Bitcoin miners announcing more than $70 billion in AI and advanced computing contracts during 2025 and early 2026, according to a first-quarter report by CoinShares.

But state and local decisions can have a more immediate impact on whether data center projects move forward. That’s because local authorities often oversee zoning and building permits and can influence access to electricity.

“A company might have access to an attractive site and a significant amount of power capacity, but if local restrictions prevent it from expanding or upgrading that infrastructure, the opportunity becomes much more difficult to pursue,” said Jordan Knecht, head of strategic integrations at Kresus, a blockchain infrastructure company.

Last week, voters backed 27 measures to limit or oppose new data centers in the elections, according to Ballotpedia. These included five complete bans, 12 partial bans and six-month pauses on new projects in three Maine towns.

In Grove City, Ohio, voters approved a rule requiring public approval for large projects that use more than 20 megawatts of electricity, among other conditions.

“Federal policy will certainly influence the broader investment environment, but the practical ability to develop and operate these facilities often comes down to conditions in individual markets,” Knecht told Cointelegraph. “For miners looking to transition into AI, I think understanding those local dynamics will be just as important as following developments in Washington.”

Building AI-ready data center infrastructure can cost between $8 million and $15 million per megawatt, compared with $700,000 to $1 million per megawatt for traditional mining facilities, according to a 2026 report by CoinShares.

That gap helps explain why a site with power already secured is only a starting point for miners moving into AI. “Transitioning from Bitcoin mining to AI workloads requires significant additional investment in infrastructure for cooling, networking, and reliability,” Knecht said.

Yuliya Barabash, founder of SBSB Fintech Lawyers, also pointed to differences in electricity needs between Bitcoin mining and AI data centers. Bitcoin miners can often reduce electricity use when prices rise, while AI data centers typically need a consistent power supply, she said.

“Higher electricity prices and additional regulatory requirements could make some projects less profitable or delay their implementation,” Barabash told Cointelegraph.

This comes as electricity demand from US data centers is also expected to rise, according to the International Energy Agency (IEA). The agency estimates that data centers used 224 terawatt-hours of electricity in 2025, a figure projected to nearly double to 426 terawatt-hours by 2030.

One question facing the incoming Democratic-controlled Congress is who should pay to expand the grid for large data centers.

Knecht said lawmakers could take a closer look at how those projects affect electricity costs and how they get approved. “There may be more pressure to ensure that large data center operators are paying their fair share of infrastructure costs rather than passing those expenses on to consumers.”

Barabash also expects scrutiny of who pays for the electricity infrastructure needed to support data centers. “One of the key priorities is likely to be ensuring that technology companies, rather than ordinary consumers, bear the costs of the additional electricity infrastructure their facilities require,” she said.

Still, experts noted that closer attention does not necessarily mean Congress will discourage data center development. “AI infrastructure is becoming increasingly important to the US economy, and there is still a strong incentive to support investment in domestic computing capacity,” Knecht said.

For miners weighing a conversion, the question is whether AI revenue can justify the cost of upgrading cooling, networking and power systems. Brown thinks it can. “Miners will swap to AI because it is a better ROI than mining,” he said.

#5130. Nomura explores sale of crypto unit Laser Digital: Bloomberg

The potential sale of Laser Digital follows a fourth straight quarterly loss for Nomura’s European operations.

Japanese financial services giant Nomura Holdings is reportedly weighing a sale of its cryptocurrency subsidiary Laser Digital as Nomura’s European operations continue to post losses.

Nomura has held talks with potential buyers, including Marex Group, Bloomberg reported Wednesday. The company has yet to make a final decision and could keep the business, according to the report.

Nomura’s European businesses posted a pretax loss of 7.4 billion yen ($46.5 million) in the quarter ended Sept. 30, 2026, their fourth consecutive quarterly loss. The company posted pretax profits in every other reporting region over the same period. Nomura did not disclose whether Laser Digital, which is headquartered in Switzerland, contributed to the European result.

Nomura is one of Japan’s largest financial services groups, with its investment management division overseeing 152.8 trillion yen ($960 billion) in assets as of September 2026.

The potential sale comes less than three months after Laser Digital Japan secured the country’s first new crypto exchange license in four years, allowing the firm to expand its institutional crypto trading services in Japan.

Established in 2022, Laser Digital provides institutional cryptocurrency trading, asset management and venture investment services. The company has also pursued expansion in the United States, receiving preliminary approval from the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency for its proposed national trust bank in May.

During a January earnings call, chief financial officer Hiroyuki Moriuchi said the company would reduce its cryptocurrency trading positions to limit short-term volatility while continuing to expand Laser Digital over the medium to long term.

#5129. A High-Severity Bug Could Have Stalled Bitcoin Mining—Bitcoin Core Has a Fix

A High-Severity Bug Could Have Stalled Bitcoin Mining—Bitcoin Core Has a Fix

Bitcoin Core developers have disclosed three security vulnerabilities in the software that runs most of the Bitcoin network, including one high-severity bug that could disrupt mining, all of which have since been fixed.

Bitcoin Core, the reference software operating the majority of the network’s nodes, classifies vulnerabilities into four tiers, from “low” to “critical,” and these fell into the middle of that range.

The most serious, tracked as CVE-2025-62861, concerns block template creation, the process miners rely on to assemble new blocks.

According to the advisory, a chain reorganization could trigger an erroneous eviction of certain transactions, corrupting a node’s mempool, its waiting room for unconfirmed transactions, so that an affected node could no longer build block templates until it was restarted. For a miner, that means a halt to the very work that earns block rewards.

A second flaw, CVE-2025-62859, could let a peer stall a node’s block processing by announcing a compact block with a manipulated witness, a form of transaction malleation.

None of the three rises to the “critical” level Bitcoin Core reserves for bugs that threaten coin theft at the protocol level, unauthorized coin creation or permanent network-wide chain splits. The developers did not report any evidence the flaws were exploited.

The disclosures come amid a broader reckoning over how quickly Bitcoin’s defenders can keep pace with a shifting threat landscape.

Bitcoin Core recently rewrote its security policy to account for AI, adding a category for “easily discoverable” vulnerabilities that can be surfaced through a simple AI prompt, which it now patches and discloses immediately rather than holding under a long embargo.

That shift reflects mounting concern that large language models have lowered the bar for finding flaws, a worry underscored by the volunteer “Bitcoin Red Team,” which has been racing to uncover AI-discoverable bugs across the ecosystem before attackers can exploit them.

The fixes themselves shipped earlier, with the advisories following under Bitcoin Core’s disclosure timeline. Node operators are generally urged to run the latest software to stay protected.

#5127. Uncalled US House races could send more crypto allies to Congress

The remaining races feature pro-crypto candidates and others without a stated position, giving advocacy groups what they see as “an opportunity” for outreach.

Just over a week after a “blue wave” handed Democrats control of the US House and Senate, 14 races have yet to be called. The results so far have added crypto supporters in both chambers and the remaining races could bring more into Congress.

Three candidates in the uncalled races received backing from crypto political action committees (PACs). According to Stand With Crypto, every candidate in those races is either strongly pro-crypto or has no stated position.

As crypto advocacy groups look ahead to 2028, they’re turning their attention to candidates who have yet to take a public stance on crypto.

Crypto PACs backed three candidates in US House races that remain undecided. Two incumbents lead in Alaska and North Carolina, while a Louisiana state senator faces a December runoff.

Louisiana state Sen. Blake Miguez will face Democrat Chauna Banks in a Dec. 12 runoff for the state’s 6th District. The Fellowship PAC backed Miguez with $250,000.

The Associated Press called the race in South Carolina’s 2nd District for incumbent Rep. Joe Wilson on the morning of Nov. 11. Stand With Crypto rates Wilson as “strongly supportive of crypto.” His win brought the group’s House tally to 246 crypto supporters and 107 opponents, while the Senate stood at 50 supporters and 33 opponents.

In the remaining races, Stand With Crypto lists each candidate as either strongly supportive of crypto or having no stated position. “The new members that have no stance present an opportunity,” Blockchain Association Chief Policy Officer Lindsay Fraser told Cointelegraph.

Nearly one in four members of the incoming Congress will be new, Fraser said, and many have given little thought to blockchain technology. “To us, that represents a blank slate in terms of education,” she said.

Blockchain Association is planning a January fly-in to reach newly elected lawmakers. “Leaving the 120th Congress with more members in that A and B category would be a huge success,” she said, referring to Stand With Crypto’s ratings. The group gives an A to candidates who strongly support crypto and a B to those who are somewhat supportive.

Fraser said age plays a bigger role than party affiliation in how lawmakers vote on crypto. She said members under 55 are much more likely to support the industry and that the incoming class skews younger.

Fraser said she wants the industry to enter the 2028 election “with two candidates who […] are thoughtful in regards to the industry,” so the sector can avoid “living and dying by the outcome of that election.”

#5126. What Are Solana Privacy Rings? Inside Helius’ Privacy Protocol for Solana

Every transaction on Solana is public by default. Anyone can look up a wallet address and see its balance, what it has sent and received, and when. That transparency is useful for verifying the network, but a problem for anyone who doesn't want their finances on display: a business paying salaries, a trader building a position, or an individual who simply doesn't want strangers seeing their savings.

Rings are programmable, onchain environments for encrypting money, markets, and finance - without ever leaving Solana

Now live on mainnet. pic.twitter.com/ezCIUCjcbI

Rings support SOL and other tokens on Solana, including SPL and Token-2022 tokens. Users keep custody of their funds throughout, since every transaction needs the owner's signature.

Crucially, the private balances live on the same network as everything else. Private transactions execute directly on Solana and encrypted data is stored on Solana's ledger, with no separate layer-2 network or sequencer in between. That means private balances sit alongside the same stablecoins, tokenized assets and DeFi liquidity as public ones.

Each private transfer comes with a zero-knowledge proof, a piece of cryptography that lets the network confirm the transfer is valid, for example that the sender actually had the funds, without revealing the numbers involved. Rings use a type of zero-knowledge proof called a Groth16 zk-SNARK, which Helius says can be generated in tens of milliseconds, either by a service provider or on the user's own device. Each private transfer settles in a single Solana transaction.

Users read their own encrypted balances with a viewing key. They can decrypt their balance locally, or let a service provider decrypt it and serve it to them, and they can share a key for a single transaction with an auditor or counterparty without exposing the rest of their history.

Moving funds into a Ring from an ordinary public wallet, or back out again, is visible onchain, including the asset and amount. But users who on-ramp from fiat currency straight into a private balance, through a supported provider, keep the asset and amount hidden from the start, according to Helius.

Because Custom Rings interoperate with the default Ring, regulated firms can keep their activity confidential from the market while still sharing the same liquidity as everyone else.

Private transfers are just the start. Solana programs can hold encrypted balances in private escrows and release them when set conditions are met, opening the way for:

At launch, Decrypt, mining and staking app ORE and cross-chain service Aurora Intents were using the default Ring, while Backpack, DFlow, Jupiter, Squads and Titan were among the teams working on integrations.

Helius says the core programs and zero-knowledge circuits have been audited by security firms Certora, OtterSec and ZK Security. Engineers from the Solana Foundation, Blueshift, Zcash and other teams took part in the "trusted setup," the one-off ceremony that generates the parameters the proofs rely on.

The code is open source, published on GitHub under the Apache 2.0 license, so anyone can inspect how it works.

Formal verification, a mathematical check that the circuits behave exactly as intended, is still underway and expected to finish by the end of 2026. Solana's own documentation currently describes Rings as being in beta.

#5124. Ethlabs proposes advancing Ethereum’s post-quantum to 2027, citing AI acceleration

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The group said it aims to make Ethereum post-AI by 2027 as well, resistant to cryptographic risks that AI development is bringing.

Ethlabs proposed bringing forward Ethereum's Ethereum (ETH) post-quantum upgrade to 2027, arguing that accelerating quantum timelines makes earlier action necessary.

In a post on the Ethereum Magicians forum, Ethlabs said the upgrade — which it calls "pqai27" — is needed because AI is accelerating the timelines for quantum and cryptographic risks, while also creating an opportunity for Ethereum to lock in its position as a credible decentralized root of trust.

Ethlabs noted that the U.S. government moved its quantum risk goal from 2035 to 2030 and that Google now aims to be post-quantum by 2029. The Ethereum Foundation previously established December 2029 for Ethereum's full post-quantum readiness.

"Being post-quantum is not enough; we intend on being post-AI too," Ethlabs wrote. It defined post-AI as cryptography that rests on as few assumptions as possible, and clients hardened against frontier models that find bugs at scale, using formal verification and Ethereum's client diversity.

It also pointed to AI solving hundreds of open mathematics problems and finding roughly 1,000 vulnerabilities in Linux, noting that "the security of our cryptography is itself an open maths problem."

"You don't have to believe in any single one of these threats," Ethlabs said. "What has changed is that it has become much harder to reason when any of them will arrive. Even at today's levels, AI is accelerating all of them."

It distinguished two levels of readiness for 2027: "pqai-ready," where consensus is post-quantum, and users have a post-quantum route for their accounts while the network continues at scaled capacity, and "pqai-scaled," in which the network operates at full capacity with post-quantum transactions and data availability.

Moving the target timeline to 2027 will "future-proof" Ethereum's position as "the credible decentralized root of trust" for the global economy, the proposal said, adding that its resilience against AI-led uncertainty will be Ethereum's next killer feature.

"With attacks on software taking off, security and uptime will be more in demand than ever," the proposal said. "Now is the cheapest time to commit. A pivot later, when we are deep into shipping the currently planned fork, would be far more costly than today."

Ethlabs said Ethereum already has a clear technical path, built around hash-function-based cryptography with minimal assumptions, years of prior research, a dedicated post-quantum team, and embedded formal verification practices.

To meet the 2027 target, Ethlabs said upcoming hard forks, including Hegotá, should be reassessed so that anything not on the critical path to post-quantum and post-AI readiness is reconsidered. The group stated it will withdraw quick slots, a proposal to make the network's block production faster, from Hegotá and dedicate its resources to pqai27.

"We don't take this decision lightly," it wrote. "We pushed for quick slots because our survey showed that the community clearly wants them, and we are ready to defend this decision to the people who trusted us to deliver them."

Founded by former Ethereum Foundation researchers, Ethlabs launched earlier this year as a nonprofit organization for Ethereum research and development.
